Each of these has five,
And of the root and the branches,
Possessing twelve and eight,
They are the supreme wealth to be practiced.
To the child who practices yoga,
Having examined with certainty the continuum of the mind of awakening,
For a mind of the profound, perfect, supreme bliss,
This scripture of certainty is taught.
This is the pledge that goes before;
Prior to accomplishing the profound,
It is the fierce vajra pledge.
In order to guard the perilous, narrow paths,
If one guards the general and the particular samayas
With the definitive meaning,
That is a great vessel of riches.
Thus, abiding in the general vows,
At this juncture of time,
What is to be guarded and kept is this:
The lion is not to be destroyed,
For it is the king of beasts.
The casket is a treasury of desires;
It is not right to put poison in it.
The tender shoots of the sprout of great awareness
Are not to be cut.
Boiled snowmelt
Is not to be drunk by the wise.
The lotus anthers that have not opened
Are not to be opened except by the moonbeams.
A vessel unexamined and full of holes
Is not to be opened, nor the elixir poured in.
Counterfeit tokens without meaning
Are not to be kept and used.
Precious eggs of white crystal
Are not to be churned in a muddy pit.
The milk of the white lioness
Is not poured into a poisonous vessel.
The precious wish-fulfilling jewel
Is never to be burned.
The great garuḍa's wings, spread in the sky,
Are not to be broken.
The fiery mass of meteoric iron
Is not to be struck.

The remains of the kill made
By the tearing of tiger and leopard are not to be gathered up.
The foundation packed with great vajra rocks
Is not to be demolished.
The close-knit armor of the vajra fence
Is not to be loosened from its tightness.
The lamp that burns the firewood
Is not to be stirred by the wind of distraction.
The flowing stream of vajra words
Is not to be cut off midway.
The king's seal, the token of his command,
Is never to be effaced, not even in passing.
The fortress packed with vajra diamond
Is not to be overturned from its foundation.
The blazing crest jewel
Is not to be taken down from the tip of the victory banner.
These are the samayas of the fortunate
Who are distinguished in the activity of yoga,
The supreme wealth of those endowed with fortune.
The supreme wealth to be practiced continuously
Is the strict sessions of vajra activity.
With the great wrath of vajra armor and dagger,
The enemy that obstructs life,
The dense afflictions, is to be destroyed.
The torpor that raises the army of laziness,
The obstinacy that blocks, the ground of pride,
The intoxicating elixir that makes the faculties arrogant,
The conditions that make one bold, these too are to be restrained.
Apart from the vajra secret words,
The talk of mixed persons and of the childish
Is not to be uttered, not even in part;
All is to be done in vajra secrecy.
Just as men who are to be killed,
When the executioner comes to kill them,
Strive for a means of escape,
So conquer the enemy of laziness and exert yourself in diligence.
To clear away the obstacles that obscure yoga:
Those knotted up in austerities and vows,
Those at odds in desires and enjoyments,
Those in general agreement who are not one within,
The two-faced and the harmful,
Those drunk with desire, sluggish, torpid, and greedy,
The faithless, those whose root is broken, the flighty and distracted:
These the wise should diligently shun.
They are obstacles to yoga;
They cut off the river of accomplishments.
For the one who practices yoga with certainty,
Five obstructing māras arise:
The māra of restless thoughts,
Likewise the māra of indifference and laziness,
The māra of distraction by wealth and bustle,
The māra of harsh words, sharp weapons,
And the māra of wrath, ferocity, short temper, and strife.
These five māras of yogic practice
Obscure the true meaning;
With the antidote, the supreme mind of awakening,
Turn them back into the state of great peace.
The four enemies of yogic practice
Are to be tamed by one skilled in means.
When the vajra that should be held within spreads as secret talk,
The faculty of speech is impaired,
And reproach strikes one.
That is the enemy that breaks what has been laid down.
Through inferior view and coarse, unruly conduct,
Abandoning the scripture of the command and practicing wrongly:
That is the enemy by which obstacles arise through error
In the work of those who practice with samaya foremost.
The three supreme vajras, hard to transgress,
When let slip into slack indifference,
Are like a tree with rotten roots,
A place where the great punishment of the command falls.
When one applies oneself to the work undertaken,
Through weak force of effort
The aims are not accomplished:
That is the enemy that robs, so that it bears no fruit;
For one who practices great yoga,
It is the enemy that steals the supreme wealth one desires.
The person skilled in means
Should shun these four enemies, the faults.
To realize these fields, to be liberated throughout the three existences,
As buddha fields
Is to train in the grounds all at once;
It is the supreme purification of fields.
These causes of offering, the three realms,
Are the supreme great requisites,
The cause that pleases all the Victors,
A marvelous offering.
These places, saṃsāra as a charnel ground,
Are the abode of great bliss.
In the unmistaken, unfabricated expanse of phenomena,
Spontaneously present without seeking,
The roots in which the five poisons move
Are the roots of the five wisdoms:
All-knowing, all-aware, and luminous.
Self-aware wisdom arises from itself.
The yogin who has the eye of Dharma
Views it thus in the definitive meaning.
This is the continuous samaya,
The guarding of the inexpressible.
As for being able to guard it,
The special prowess of samaya
Is taught as a scripture of certainty
For the minds of those who hold awareness.
Attachment through desire, destroying,
Delusion, pride, and jealousy
Are the samayas of great yoga,
To be practiced by a person possessed of the meaning.
Union and liberation,
Taking what is not given, and emanated speech:
The supreme of practices of yogic samaya,
These are deeds to be practiced.
The Victors and all sentient beings,
And all that is imputed as things,
Are one in the state of the meaning;
That itself is the lord of body, speech, and mind.
All phenomena that are signs
Are clear within the state of great equality.
The distinctions of the lower vehicles
All are clear to the self-aware mind.
Not proclaiming whatever qualities arise,
And not disparaging those below,
Is the samaya of the equality of all;
If one knows it thus, the supreme accumulations are perfected.
Forms are Vairocana;
Words that are uttered are Amitābha;
Thoughts of every kind are the Vajra of Mind;
Awareness and feeling are Ratnasambhava;
The movement of the mind is Sarvārthasiddhi (Amoghasiddhi).
The seat and its nature are the place of practice.
When endowed with the ten fields,
This is the enjoyment of great bliss,
And unobstructed activity is perfected in its four kinds.
Outer objects and the inner mind
All abide as equality itself.
Of this, the King of Equality
Never spoke of obscured and unobscured.
Since all has become the expanse of equality itself,
The five aggregates are in truth the five wisdoms;
The lord of body, speech, and mind
Is the vidyādhara of the great seal.
Since there is no nature other than
The nature of object and wisdom,
However the aspects of signs may appear,
There is no ground for any penalty in that.
If one asks what the sacred samaya is:
Sacred realization is samaya;
Whatever brings realization,
That is the great samaya.
Abiding in the state of the equality of all,
Out of the miraculous display of meditative absorption,
Perform all conduct in actuality;
That is the great samaya.
What has become the vessel is the expanse of the Mother;
Its contents are the sky of the Victor, the Father;
Enjoy their arising and entering with awareness:
This is the vajra pledge.
All the sentient beings of existence
And all the categories of the teachings of liberation
All appear each in its own way,
Yet all are clear in the state of equality.
The deeds of conduct, and realization,
And all that has become signs,
That outer and inner domain of the mind,
When examined, becomes an accumulation of enjoyment.
Unborn emptiness is the seal of body;
Natural wishlessness is the meaning of speech;
The signless, beyond the mind, is the heart;
These three are the buddha of great bliss.

When one holds sway through the view of equality,
All of saṃsāra appears within the expanse.
This is the supreme completion of all actions;
No activity remains to be done elsewhere.
Suffering is great awakening;
Saṃsāra is primordially beyond sorrow.
The obscurations are the utterly pure expanse of phenomena;
All the afflictions, too, are the meaning of awakening.
Seeing the meaning that is without appearance,
In the sequence of the grounds
There is nothing to grasp as "this" and "this."
This is the state of attaining non-appearance.
One abides in the state of suchness itself,
With body, speech, and mind unmoving.
However it may be transformed, since it is the nature of reality,
It is free of being called a ground for penalty.
Nonexistence, openness, singularity,
And spontaneous perfection are the great samayas.
Not confined to a side as "this":
This is the samaya of guarding.
Since there is nothing practiced and nothing to be practiced,
All that appears as the conduct
Of existence, of non-Buddhists (tīrthikas), and of worldly people
Is spontaneously present enlightened conduct.
Since what is unabandoned and spontaneously present are indivisible,
There is no object of view other than the expanse.
This is the samaya of view.
Earth, water, fire, and wind, together with the vessel and its contents,
All are by nature the nature of reality.
The nature of reality, without anything other in its meaning,
Is luminous, self-arisen wisdom.
The precious wish-fulfilling jewel
Is present in oneself without being sought.
It is the enjoyment from which desires come forth;
It is the accumulation of the enjoyment of great bliss.
All the lower realms and higher realms,
And all the phenomena of the noble path and of saṃsāra,
Are not apprehended as natures separate from one another;
They are the very nature of the buddha.
All conduct and all kinds of form
Are the nature of the great seal.
Body, speech, mind, and words teach extensively;
This is the nature of the mind that knows the meaning.
Unborn, unarisen,
Nonabiding, there is no attachment to it.
When one fully realizes the special quality of the meaning,
It is the supreme ground of Samantabhadra.
The major and minor marks are all complete;
All is the meaning of body, speech, and mind;
All is perfected in the great self.
Since all is spread out in the great pervasion
Of spontaneously perfect great bliss,
All obscurations are purified and all accumulations are perfected.
Liberated, free of all bonds,
It is the great abode of spontaneously present bliss.

Thus spoke the great Lion of Speech.

From the great king of tantras, the Vajra Array, the Sūtra of the All-Gathering Awareness, this is the fourth chapter, called "The Supreme Secret of Samaya."

Then again Sattvavajra entered into equipoise in the meditative absorption called "Indicating All Phenomena through Symbols." Then, when he had risen from that meditative absorption, the retinue of the Great Assembly said: "Great being, we see all phenomena as having the essence of mere symbolic understanding."

Then the great being declared: "Host of the Great Assembly, know all phenomena to be mere symbol, convention, and designation. All designated phenomena abide nowhere, neither outside nor inside. All phenomena have no basis for names and are free of designated conventions. What is called a symbol is a convention designated for what is hidden.

So that the hidden teachings remain secret,
All should be concealed and transmitted.
In this way the yogin
Should come to know the application of symbols."

Then the yogins of the assembly said: "Great being, please explain the teachings of symbols extensively.

We too will transmit
All teachings through the meaning of symbols.
What is it that is called a symbol?
What are the hidden teachings?
What are the secrets not to be revealed?"

When they had requested this, the Lion of Speech declared:

"Listen, host of the Great Assembly!
What is called a symbol is hidden speech.
The hidden, secret teachings
Are the conduct of great yoga.
What is not revealed and kept secret from others:
Because non-Buddhists and other groups
Deride yogic conduct,
In order to be most careful and keep it hidden,
It should be taught through hidden symbols.
The great qualities of symbols,
Though inconceivable even in number,
Are fourfold when summarized:
They are not the domain of others;
They are the domain of yogic awareness;
Through hiddenness, accomplishment is close at hand;
And the secret samaya is not broken,
And they become a means of closeness and affection
For the mothers and sisters who delight in symbols.
Therefore the hidden symbols
Should be expressed, indicated by words:

a la la hoḥ a ti pu hoḥ pra ti bu hoḥ mahā go ru saṃ kra ma gu ya de bī samaya mahā candra mahā purśa gu ya bha ga mahā vajra samaya hoḥ samaya stvaṃ ra ho ya haṃ ra ga yā mi samaya jaḥ lhan oṃ āṃḥ hūṃ samaya samaya e hye hi e hye hi ha he ha ha he ha ma taṃ kha ma taṃ kha ka rukṣa ka rukṣa kṣaṃ ba ri kṣaṃ ba ri ra ho haṃ ra ho haṃ samaya hi a la stoṃ a la stoṃ kha kha le kha le śa da pa śa da pa kha raṃ khā hi kha raṃ kha hi kha le bhyo kha le ha bhyo kha ka kha kha ka kha kha hyi ha he rakṣa khā hi rakṣa khā hi ha ha he raṃ guhya guhya sama sama ma le mu le ta le du le ma ca me ca guhya sitti parama parama śi ri hoḥ śi ri ho ma ma kha ka traṃ e e e e hriṃ hriṃ bhyo bhyo kha kha le le karma ma ti bhyo rakṣa kra ma bhyo rakṣa kra ma bhyo karma ma le karma ma le kṣu ti śa ne kṣu ti śa ne rakṣa karma rakṣa karma krodha kṣaṃ khā hi bhyo kha hi bhyo ca le yaṃ ca le yaṃ pra ca le pra ca le gu rā yaṃ gu rā yaṃ bi gurtti bi gurtti mamo'haṃ mamo'haṃ

This is the seal of the samaya of verbal symbols.

byā ghri carma byā ghri carma ba yaṃ kṣaṃ ga ri ba yaṃ kṣaṃ ga ri ba byā jño ba ba ti ba byā jño ba ba ti na ra ha sa na ra ha sa bandha ka pā la bandha ka pā la carma na ga truṃ carma na ga truṃ rā ga rakta ca ra ga rakta ca ru dhi rā ma ta ru dhi ra ma ta ka ru ga ca ka ru ga ca ba bya ba ti ba bya ba ti gaṃ gro ṇa gaṃ gro ṇa tharpa ta tharpa ta

Courage-maker, kha! Courage-maker, kha!
The waxing moon and the full moon,
The white moon and the supreme moonbeam,
The chief of all who hold life,
The gentle one, the great liberation,
The supreme taste called gal,
The sweet fragrance of great splendor,
The maidservant and the female attendant,
The sharp dagger of Mañjuśrī,

mā tha ya mā tha ya parpa ti parpa ti rva tha ma rva tha ma kha taṃ ña kha taṃ ña rva ti hūṃ rva ti hūṃ śi ñaṃ tha śi ñaṃ tha ka raṃ tha ka raṃ tha śakti kha śakti kha yo gac rbān yo ga carbān

This is the seal of the samaya of sound symbols.

As for the great mudrā of the hands:
Circling the hands in dance to the right and left,
Then snapping the fingers is the sign for inviting.
Circling, then joining the palms, is the sign of the mudrā of homage.
Circling fully, then releasing at the crown of the head,
Is the sign of accepting offerings.
Circling, then holding the hands at the heart,
Is the sign of firm vajra samādhi.
Circling in dance and fully presenting both hands
Is the sign of making offerings.
Circling in dance and placing the hand on the tip of the ring finger
Is the sign of entering into the meaning of union.
Method places it at the tip,
And wisdom at the middle of the ring finger.
If method puts a finger to the right secret place,
For wisdom the secret place is the ear.
If method raises the hand from the right,
Wisdom raises it from the left.
Method displays the vajra mudrā;
Wisdom displays the lotus mudrā.
If method moves the right eyebrow,
Wisdom should raise the left eyebrow.
Method stretches out the left hand,
And wisdom, with the right, shows the manner of embracing.
Circling the mudrā in dance, clapping the palms,
And touching the ring finger to the tongue
Is the sign that the time for the feast offering has come.
Circling both hands, then flicking with the ring finger,
And then touching the ring finger to the tongue,
Is the signal for passing around affection and laughter.
These samayas of signs, such as the mahā, the great kiṃkara and the karma,
And the act of holding the sign of vajra heat,
Are accomplished when the yogin practices them.
This is the seal of the samaya of bodily signs.

Thus spoke the Lion of Speech.

This concludes the fifth chapter, on the signs of yoga, from the great king of tantras, the Vajra Array, the Sūtra of the All-Gathering Awareness.

Then again the Lion of Speech, Sattvavajra, entered into equipoise in the samādhi called the Gathering of Yogic Play. When he had arisen from that samādhi, the gathering of the Great Assembly said, "Great Lion of Speech, we see all phenomena in the maṇḍala of the vajra gathering." When they had said this, the Lion of Speech declared, "Gathering of the Great Assembly, know that the intrinsic nature of all phenomena is the vajra gathering." When he had declared this, the assembled gathering said, "Great Being, please explain to us at length the meaning of the maṇḍala of the vajra gathering, which is the intrinsic nature of all phenomena." The Great Being declared:

Listen, gathering of the Great Assembly!
This maṇḍala of the vajra gathering
Is the heart commitment of the Victors,
The path practiced by all yogins.
These paths that traverse the supreme grounds
I shall explain to the assembled gathering.
When a beginner who has just set out
Enters the vajra gathering,
In accordance with the particular stages,
Through the nature of this supreme action,
Having trained step by step as is fitting,
To proceed to the most supreme ground
One should advance by five supreme methods:
One should apply the vajra gathering, approach,
Close approach, accomplishment,
And great accomplishment.
In keeping with the numbers, the great occasions,
And whatever is accompanied by the arising of signs,
Applying the hidden means of concealment,
One should employ the great method that no one can detect.
They are the lords of secrets.
The childish who enter this place,
Having been drawn out of the dust of the world,
Should be set on the ground where change and mastery are not yet certain.
Why so? Because they are beginners,
Childish ones whose mental strength has not yet unfolded,
They do not know good from bad or happiness from suffering,
And cannot tell apart the aims of the open teaching and the secret.
At this command door of the practice of yoga
The secret door should not be opened, for they would become frightened.
Because, from among childish ordinary beings
Who have not realized the intrinsic nature and its disposition,
Māra himself, making a vessel of one who is no vessel,
Harms the yoga and cuts off its life,
And through uncertain turning back and entering,
This secret would be laid bare.
Therefore its conduct too
Should be applied by easeful means, as is fitting.

Then the Lion of Speech entered into equipoise in the samādhi of the vajra gathering of method, and the great yoginīs became invisible. Then the assembled gathering said, "Great Lion of Speech, we see the intrinsic nature of all phenomena as the nature of the mudrā of method." The Sattva declared, "These gatherings of the Great Assembly should be known as gatherings handed down in lineage and concealed."

The deities endowed with the vajra yoga gathering,
The five concealed gatherings of the mudrā of great method,
Together with the sixteen, Vajradhara and the rest:
This vajra maṇḍala
Should be drawn, or else visualized,
Or recited, or consecrated, or set upon a sign.
By the Dharma tradition of the Lord of the World,
Having generated and recollected it for the sake of devotion,
And having brought it down from the realm of its intrinsic nature,
With offerings such as prostration,
And with great articles such as vajra medicine and nectar mixed together,
One should present them to the gathering of vajra method.
Singing the songs of vajra yoga,
Having pleased them and completed the number for accomplishing the activities,
One should make offerings and fulfill the samaya as one wishes.
To disciples who are suitable vessels,
Through acts such as heroic knowing,
One grants the mode of entry, empowerment and accomplishments.
With the five kinds of vajra oath,
Having conferred the duties of training as appropriate,
In stages according to ability,
One has them swear the momentous vajra oath.
Then, for the greater and lesser accomplishment practices,
As they wish, with diligence,
The vajra yoga should be enjoined as a command.
With samayas that do not permit saying "I cannot" and the like,
One should be strict.
Generating the resolve to keep the secret from the deluded,
One transforms their perception with words of wonder.
Once they understand, the objects of knowledge
Should all be shown to their eyes step by step.
To the intelligent one in whom warmth has coiled,
Open the door of the inner treasury of yoga.
To the wild unruly one of coarse conduct,
Show the dangerous straits and distractions of saṃsāra.
To one who has the measure of vajra scripture,
Grant empowerment according to capacity.
To one of incapable disposition who does not seek,
Only what is beneficial is to be praised.
In this place, whether it is present or absent,
Do not efface the radiance, and do not lower the weight.
To one with the disposition of vajra intellect,
One should give the instruction, "Train in great skill."
In the empowerment row of the vajra assembly,
Examine the great downfalls regarding the vajra meaning.
Distinguish the particulars of skill and stage.
The mudrās of the respective families
Are given either in common or else one by one.

Then the Lion of Speech entered into equipoise in the samādhi called the Gathering of the Dhāraṇī of Wisdom. When he had arisen from that samādhi, the assembled gatherings said this:

"We see the intrinsic nature of all phenomena residing in the maṇḍala of the wisdom gathering." When they had said this, the Great Being declared, "Gathering of the Great Assembly, know that all phenomena are awakened in the maṇḍala of the wisdom gathering. Why is that? All phenomena arise from the intrinsic nature of wisdom, and they are clear as the intrinsic nature of wisdom."

Then the assembled gathering said, "Great Being, please explain to us at length the intrinsic nature of the dhāraṇī of wisdom." When they had said this, the Great Being declared:

Gathering of the great yoga of the assembly,
Understand the intrinsic nature
Of the dhāraṇī of wisdom through my extensive explanation.
Just as the gatherings were joined through method,
Those endowed with yoginīs,
Vajradhāriṇī and the rest,
Are joined into a gathering of goddesses.
Here, entering, joy and the rest,
The mudrās of method, are concealed within signs.
The dhāraṇīs of vajra equality
Should be pleased with offerings of equality.
Through dance, song lyrics and the like,
One accomplishes the vidyādhara yoginīs.

Thus he declared.

Then again Sattvavajra entered into equipoise in the samādhi in which all phenomena, method and wisdom, are linked with one another as in a net. When he had arisen from that samādhi, the retinue said, "Blessed One, we see the intrinsic nature of all phenomena as having the nature of being linked as method and wisdom." When they had said this, the Blessed One declared, "Yogins of the Great Assembly, know that all phenomena are linked as method and wisdom, and are nondual."

Then the yogins of the assembly said, "Great Being, please explain the meaning of all phenomena being linked as a net." The Great Being declared:

The meaning of linkage is this:
The characteristic of all phenomena
Appears in the manner of mutual linkage.
From the space-like characteristic of all phenomena,
Ten million emanations appear as anything at all.
They are linked in the manner of a nondual net.
This is the supreme secret of yoga.
The skillful guardian of the secret door
Should open the secret door to it.
To the sons who have entered this secret
Proclaim this command of the vajra secret.
In this sacred secret maṇḍala,
The Vajra King together with the Queen,
The five themselves entwined as couples,
Join in embrace, entwining from right to left.
On the right is the vajra of awakening,
With each one's own emblem blazing.
On the left, the sweet-sounding bell,
Clear and beautiful with its signs;
Radiant, swaying and bowing,
In a manner of passion and movement,
Upon great lions, supreme bulls and the like,
They sit poised in a perfect manner,
Blazing with the light of five colors,
Graceful in the sixteen adornments.
Generate them gradually or all at once,
Applying this according to the grades of faculties.
The retinue of the secret gathering, too,
With natures transformed at their very base,
Mudrā kings together with queens,
Eight pairs coupled, entwined in embrace:
Kṣitigarbha and Ākāśagarbha, Avalokiteśvara, Vajrapāṇi,
Maitreya and Vajra, Samantabhadra and Mañjuśrī.
The inner and outer articles,
Joining and pleasing through the two bases,
The visualization and so forth, are likewise.
Together with the vajra gatekeepers,
This is the secret gathering of vajra embrace.
By joining the great enjoyments of yoga,
Offer in sequence to please and to fulfill.
Through the secret mode of entry and the like
One accomplishes the secret aims.

Thus he declared.

Then the Blessed One, Sattvavajra, entered into equipoise in the samādhi called the Expanse of Vajra All-Linking. Then the assembled retinue said this to the Blessed One: "We see the intrinsic nature of all phenomena in the expanse of the vajra net." The Blessed One declared, "Gathering of the Great Assembly, know it as the expanse of the net. For all phenomena arise from the awareness of the heart, and the intrinsic nature of the heart is nothing but vajra."

The assembled gathering said, "Blessed One, please explain to us at length the gathering of the great vajra net." The Blessed One declared:

Yogins of the Great Assembly,
The meaning of great vajra awareness,
The very expanse of the linked net,
I shall explain with certainty; listen one-pointedly.
Because this awareness, the great secret of the heart,
Is the definitive vajra,
And because the meaning of the linkage of vajra faculties and objects
All arises from it, know it as the expanse.
Vajradhāriṇī, the holders and the rest,
Are likewise entwined in equality.
The images abide as the nature of samādhi.
Having joined them in the proper sequence,
Do as you wish.

Thus he declared.

Then again the Blessed One, Sattvavajra, entered into equipoise in the samādhi called the Equality of All Four Times. When he had arisen from that samādhi, the gathering of the Great Assembly said this: "Blessed One, we see all phenomena as one in the equality of the four times." When they had said this, the Blessed One declared, "Gathering of the Great Assembly, all phenomena are realized as the equality of the four times. Know that all phenomena are perfected within the state of the four times. Why is that? In the state of equality, no earlier or later time appears."

Then the assembled gatherings said, "Blessed One, please explain to us at length the very time of great equality." When they had said this, the Blessed One declared:

Gathering of the Great Assembly,
If one knows the time of equality,
Even the name called "time" does not exist.
If one fully knows the four times as equal,
That is the intrinsic nature of the equality of the nature of reality.
The gathering of goddesses of vajra time,
The queens of equality who hold sway
Over the portions of the past, the present,
And what has not yet come,
In destruction, birth, ripening,
And cessation:
Vajra liberation and the immeasurables,
Mind, and likewise the wisdom retinue
Of equality and the center of glory.
In the same way, in sequence, with vajra
Song, dance and other articles,
Make offerings and follow the mode of entry as above,
Without letting the portions pass beyond their times,
On the days of the deities.

Great Bliss and the rest,
Whether as clusters or as mudrās,
Should be applied in common or one by one.
For the past, urge on the gathering of liberation;
For the present and what has not yet come,
Bring about equality with immeasurable mind.
One then becomes a king of equality,
And becomes a Sugata, a king of time.

Thus he declared.

Then again the Blessed One, Sattvavajra, entered into equipoise in the samādhi called the Gathering of Sun and Moon. When he had arisen again from that samādhi, the gathering of the Great Assembly said this: "Blessed One, we see all phenomena as a gathering of sun and moon." When they had said this, the Blessed One declared, "Gathering of the Great Assembly, know that the intrinsic nature of all phenomena is the nature of the mind of awakening and great compassion. All phenomena arise from the mind of awakening and have the nature of compassion."

Then the assembled gathering said, "Blessed One, please explain to us the meaning of compassion and the mind of awakening." The Blessed One declared:

The equipment of all phenomena
Is compassion and the mind of awakening.
If one is not endowed with that equipment,
The aims will not be accomplished.
The yoga in which the mind of awakening is complete
Is like the waxing moon complete in its sixteen parts.
The dawning of compassion and wisdom
Is like the clear sun rising in the sky.
The yogin in whom the two aims are complete
Belongs to the ground of definitive yoga.
To proceed to the definitive ground,
Through gatherings in the continuous flow of vajra time
One should bring about the two great accumulations.
Just as the full moon amid the host of stars
Is completed in sixteen days,
The ground of the thirteen, where great skill is perfected,
Is completed through the sixteen gatherings.
Through the sixteen beautiful days,
The moon disk, a royal seal of light,
That bliss, beautifully adorned,
Brings about merit through the sixteen gatherings.
From the half-full onward, as the accumulation increases,
The mind is trained in phenomena without reference.
Ever more peaceful, ever more without reference,
Signs and objects of knowledge are thinned away.
The accumulation of merit, too, is without reference.
When through equality it has become empty,
Everything is perfected as the dharmakāya,
And suffering and awakening are nondual.
Generating a sense of wonder at this,
One should make offerings with the moon, the times, the signs,
And the two secrets.
To those that arise in the subtle warmth,
And those that hasten forth from oneself,
In order to cut the root, by full completion,
One takes them up without doubt,
And at that very time the sun rises.
If the continuity of this time is cut off,
It is hard to turn back even through approach.
If an interruption occurs, restore and fulfill it.
When the clear moon in the sky is full,
Since the yogin on earth is full as well,
One should perform great increases of merit
And acts of great dedication.
Thereby the great fruit, too:
Riding upon the sun and moon as one goes,
Until one reaches the ground of the vidyādharas,
Great glory is accomplished on the sixteenth ground.

Thus he declared.

Then again Sattvavajra entered into equipoise in the samādhi called Abiding Continuously. When he arose, the assembled gathering said, "Blessed One, we see all phenomena abiding in the maṇḍala of the continuity of letters." The Blessed One declared, "Know that all phenomena are the nature of the maṇḍala of the Victors, and that all the Victors are the nature of the maṇḍala of letters." They said, "Please explain to us at length what the meaning of the letter mudrā is." To this request the Blessed One declared:

All phenomena are the nature of the Victors;
The Victors are clear as body, speech and mind.
Body, speech, mind and the mudrās
Are complete in the very garland of letters.
The maṇḍalas of the letter mudrās
Should be practiced continually.
Thereby one attains the continual samādhi.
The continuity of continuities is not to be cut.
At the vajra dawn and at the times of day,
Generate the gathering of letters and join the gatherings,
And likewise join them with the first portions and the remainders.
This is the path that is close to the meaning;
It swiftly brings about merit.
Through that cause, the all-complete gathering,
Whatever the authentic great gathering may be,
The sacred supreme bliss
And the great suchness, it becomes that very suchness.
With entry door, conduct and view in harmony,
All of them having become one,
Endowed with causes and conditions, excellent,
With harmony, medicine and delightful union,
Deities, vidyādharas and yogins, all of them,
Gathered without exception, should practice this Dharma.
An instant, a moment, a short while,
A brief spell, a day and night, or each month,
The auspicious days of the deities:
These times when the feast falls due
Are the great occasions of blessing.
With articles according to one's means,
Distinguished as outer and inner,
Pure food for the bhūtas,
And for the Victor free of thought and concept,
One should act diligently, according to one's means.
Self, other, and both,
And what does not become the aim of the Dharma:
Having abandoned these four faults,
And endowed with the manner that reverses them,
Regarding the yogins as one's heart and eyes,
One keeps close a feeling of tender affection
And applies oneself as one wishes.
To draw near the deities,
To pacify the obstructors,
To accomplish aims and to restore the dead,
Practicing this rite is most precious.
This feast should not be spoken of
In front of small-minded newcomers.
All the substances and articles, too,
Should be applied forcefully, in whatever way tames.
Therefore this rite, in particular,
Generating a gathering within the gathering, trained in good order,
Having become familiar with approach,
Through accomplishment the fruit is brought to its culmination.

Thus he declared.

This concludes the sixth chapter, on the gathering of the bliss of yoga, from the great king of tantras, the Vajra Array, the Sūtra of the All-Gathering Awareness.

Then again the Lion of Speech, Sattvavajra, entered into equipoise in the samādhi called Proclaiming the Past. When he had arisen from that samādhi, the gatherings of the Great Assembly said this: "Blessed One, the question has appeared in our awareness: what are the faults and the virtues of practicing the profound mantra?" Then again the Blessed One declared, "Gathering of the Great Assembly, know that the meaning of the great secret mantra is extremely difficult to practice. Why is that? The gathering of secret mantra is very much like fire. If fire is kept continually and used well, it can be used continually just as one wishes.

But if fire itself is used carelessly, it becomes a great mass of fire. It burns the very one who uses it, it burns from house to house, and it burns up every material thing. In the same way, this secret mantra is like water. If water is harnessed by skillful means and used well, one can enjoy it as one likes, and one who delights in water can even cross over a great river. But one who is not familiar with the nature of water and with the means is carried away and dies. Furthermore, this great secret mantra is like a vicious beast of prey. A beast of prey that takes the lives of other beings is brought under control by one skilled in means, just as one wishes; but if it is not mastered by means, it takes life. Furthermore, this great secret mantra is like the precious jewel that sits on the head of a snake. If the jewel on the snake's head is fully secured by skillful means, it is brought under one's control and can be used; but if it is not secured by means, it parts one from the breath of life. Therefore, because this great secret is difficult to practice, even in times past there were many who practiced it badly and followed a wrong path. And because many persons skilled in the meaning and the path, who secured it by means and practiced it well, also entered the unerring path, gathering of the Great Assembly, become fully skilled in the means of practice."

When he had declared this, the gathering of the Great Assembly said this: "Blessed One, what are the things that come from a wrong path of secret mantra? What are the things that come from the means of practicing well? Please explain them to us at length." When they had said this, the Blessed One declared to the gathering of the Great Assembly, "Gathering of the Great Assembly, I shall explain the sequence of what is wrong and what is right; listen!" And he declared:

At the time of my earliest memory,
These meanings of secret practice,
Their definitive meaning, had mostly declined.
While only a semblance of the manner remained,
Almost everyone practiced it,
Yet I did not see them meet with the meaning.
All of them practiced a wrong path.
What, then, is it to be wrong?
Not abiding in this, failing in this,
Turning from this, and practicing in a wrong manner;
What is not this manner, and is uncertain here;
A path of deceit, false honesty, and contradiction.
And how was that?
In the time of the aeon called All-Arrayed,
After that Buddha, the King of Manifest Arising,
Had passed into nirvāṇa,
The yogin Excellent Ornament who then appeared,
For a retinue of ignorant, childish ones,
Kept up a hypocritical and wrong gathering;
So, together with five hundred groups,
He strayed from the meaning and turned back into saṃsāra.
Do not practice in that manner!
This wondrous secret teaching,
At times when the authoritative lords of yoga
Are mostly not present,
Is stirred up by the ignorant with evil thoughts,
Who corrupt the word and abandon the scriptures.
Erring, they bring ruin on themselves,
Saying, "I am the one, not this other,"
Praising themselves and disparaging others: this is wrong.
And how was that?
In the times of that same aeon,
A yogin called the Giver of Courage,
Whose mental strength for the meaning had not unfolded,
And who was unskilled in the means of the path,
Abandoned the scriptures of the word of definitive meaning,
Practiced a dharma that is no Dharma,
And to others who practiced the definitive meaning
Said, "That of yours is not the meaning.
Whatever is the supreme meaning, the nectar,
That is this Dharma of mine."
Thus he praised himself and disparaged others.
This too is a wrong path of existence.
Do not practice in that manner!
If, in following a tantra that arose before,
One who lacks substance is installed as master,
Through the power of arrogance from intellectual attachment to words,
Making pronouncements without knowing,
He is hypocritical, crooked in mind,
A deranged and foolish wrongdoer.
And how was that?
A semblance of a yogin
Called the monk Stainless Conduct
Arose following one skilled in the meaning.
The retinue of a hundred thousand groups,
Who had come along following the tantra,
Laid the heavy weight of great verbal mastery
Upon that very monk who lacked the meaning,
And installed him as master, calling him the successor.
The pride of great eminence arose in him.
Not knowing, he put on a pretense,
And by practicing a counterfeit, wrong dharma,
He, together with the hundred thousand groups,
Fell into the state of Rudra of great arrogance.
Do not practice in that manner!
Patrons solicited through friendship and favors,
And a retinue living on gain and honor:
Fearing they might fall under another's control,
They cut off their doors, factions and separate camps.
"This retinue is my field to ripen,
My field for taming;
Those who are not connected with it..."
Making doors with words of affliction is an error.
And how was that?
Five hundred groups of what was called
The yogin Na Zhinépa,
Saying, "We practice the most secret mantra,"
Stayed in a remote hermitage,
And treated all the people living
In those great lands
As their field for taming.
A monk belonging to another school,
Endowed with the meaning,
Came deliberately to that land,
Intending to teach extensively
A single supreme path of definitive yoga.
But that monk who stayed in the hermitage
Issued orders in words of affliction:
"This retinue is my field;
You have no part in it."
By cutting off doors, factions and divisions,
He fell into the path of afflicted saṃsāra.
Do not practice in that manner!
Once offerings have been dedicated to the great yoga,
A few hoard whatever they can;
One mind is drawn out by another,
And never do they partake of the enjoyments.
And how was that?
In a group with what was called
The yogin Bliss-Endowed Knower,
While practicing the secret extensively,
A yoginī living in the retinue,
A woman called Excellent One,
Was appointed as the steward for the feast.
With a miserly mind she hoarded
Those articles of plenty.
Through that ripening of karma
She was born as a hungry ghost in the body of a dog.
Do not practice in that manner!
Furthermore, gathering of the great yoga,
Yogins who practice wrongly,
With wrong views, brazen, fierce and very angry,
Practicing evil and devoid of the meaning,
Still say, "I am the authentic one,"
And with careless talk steal the word, deceive and corrupt.
And how was that?
A yogin who was a Dharma protector,
One called Pramade,
Unskilled in the meaning and the path,
Proclaimed loudly to everyone,
"I am the preceptor."
He stole the word of the Victor, and through the ripening
Of having deceived beings,

That perverse worldling
Fell into the realm of the conduct of non-Buddhists (tīrthikas).
Do not practice in that manner!
With oaths broken and dull toward the meaning,
Because in arrogance they lay claim to the meaning,
Those who want the empowerment of the Vajra King
Are shameless, brazen, benighted and immoral.
And how was that?
One who practiced the manner of yogic mantra,
Called Penaya,
Appeared together with a hundred groups.
With root vows broken and dull toward the meaning,
Through the pride of great arrogance,
Because he feigned skill in the meaning,
He received the empowerment of the Vajra King.
Just as a mountain crushes living creatures,
By the mountain of pride and arrogance,
With the evil deeds of saṃsāric conduct,
He fell into the species of pig.
Do not practice in that manner!
Without realizing the very meaning of equality,
They say in words, "It is equal,"
And indulge openly in both attachment and aversion:
Through yogic action, this is the path of saṃsāra.
And how was that?
A monk called Giver of Bliss,
Together with a thousand groups,
Practiced the secret acts of yoga.
Some indulged openly in attachment;
Some, in great aversion, practiced killing.
Through the ripening of the karma of attachment and aversion,
They fell into the realm of rākṣasas.
Do not practice in that manner!
Those yogins of little intellect
Who, when the meaning of the vajra row of the gathering is discussed,
Are afraid of it and become frightened,
Lose heart, take no delight, and slink away, are fools.
And how was that?
A monk called Devadatta,
While training on the first ground of yoga,
When many yogins endowed with the meaning
Had mostly gathered together,
And the meaning of the definitive secret was discussed,
Devadatta and his retinue slipped away from it.
Taking no delight in discussing the meaning,
With an evil mind he grew jealous.
Having gone to his own dwelling,
He spoke ill of that learned one.
Through that ripening of karma,
For immeasurable aeons
He strays from the authentic meaning
And wanders in the meaningless state of saṃsāra.
Do not practice in that manner!
When a yogin himself shoots at a yogin
The poisoned arrow of extreme malice,
The oath, mighty as a fortress,
Is destroyed: the yogin is his own obstructor.
And how was that?
A learned one endowed with the meaning,
The yogin called Pure Intellect,
Came with his retinue to the place
Where one called Fearless Intellect,
Unskilled in the means and the meaning,
Looked after many communities.
Upon that learned one, endowed with the meaning and possessed of patience,
With an evil mind he cast slander and false charges.
Through that ripening of karma,
Fearless Intellect fell into the Avīci hell.
Do not practice in that manner!
Because their view lacks a focal point,
The authentic path of the Middle Way is not clear to them.
Practicing while relying on a mere general object,
That is a blind gathering of yoga.
And how was that?
There appeared a yogin
Called King Holder of Great Clarity.
Without the eye of the view of wisdom,
Adopting the manner of a yogin in name only,
He practiced scriptures known merely by hearing their sound,
And fell, eyeless, into the realm of saṃsāra.
Do not practice in that manner!
Through yogins such as these
Practicing it as a perverse gathering,
It becomes a perverse gathering of saṃsāra.
Therefore, yogins of the assembly,
The Victor has said that in these most secret teachings
There are great places for going astray.
Through conduct skilled in means
It is precious to make them meaningful.
The yogins of the good path,
Who practiced with skill in means,
The accomplished ones who arose in times gone by:
I shall tell their story.
This conduct of supreme persons,
Whoever hears the proclamation of its virtues
Gains it even through the mere name,
And by recollecting it is protected from the lower realms.
And how was that?
This most secret conduct
Was renowned in the abode of the Thirty-Three gods.
The god's son Radiant,
When the time of impermanence and death had come,
And the omens of death appeared,
Came to feel discontent.
As the perception of his next life arose,
He saw through his own higher knowledge
That he would take the body of a foolish creature
That eats filth as its food.
With a perception of extreme terror,
He brought the objects of refuge to mind.
While his memory of other things was unclear,
The name of the great gathering of the great secret
Was heard by his faculty of hearing.
By bringing what he had heard to mind,
He turned back from the path of the lower realms,
And through the good fortune of practicing the great gathering,
He was born in the abode of those who hold the secret.
In the same way, practice purely what you hear.
Whoever delights in this gathering that pleases the Victors
And develops longing for it,
Without obstacles from others,
Swiftly attains whatever he aspires to.
And how was that?
The brahmin's son Loving One,
Aspiring to practice
These gatherings of the great secret, searched everywhere.
Though he searched, he did not find them.
Sitting down in a certain place,
He wept bitterly and said,
"Alas, how my karma has failed me!
From birth until I have grown old,
I have searched for the profound meaning of the great gathering,
Yet I have not met the meaning. How evil my karma is!
How could I find it, however I search from now on?
Though I search, I will never find it.
I go for refuge to you; protect me with compassion!"
As he cried out loudly in his anguish,
A voice came forth from the expanse of the sky:
"You fortunate brahmin,
Relying on your chosen deity in this way,
You are one who has the fortune of the meaning.
All the Victors of the three times
Bestow their blessings upon you.
Therefore, without letting the view lapse,
You must be skillful and diligent
In the means of purifying the obscurations.
Having opened your own veins,
With that offering of great blood,
As sandalwood water that purifies attachment,
Visualize and offer to all the Victors,
And immediately you will attain the meaning."
Having received this prophecy,
His mind was greatly stirred with joy.
By practicing it just as prophesied,
He became a vidyādhara, a son of the Victors.
In the same way, practice your aspiration purely.
For the fortunate who see and enter here,
It is the same in every case:
Like a pinnacle ornament that has become the summit,
It is never wholly lost.
And how was that?
The one called the son of the gods,
Named King of Virtue,
Together with a hundred thousand groups,
Practiced the secret extensively.
The god's son Jewel Radiance,
Greatly stirred with faith,
Went to that secret place
Bringing many divine substances and articles.
With joy and longing,
He became reverent and stayed there,
And so he gained four great miraculous powers:
Traveling to all the abodes of the gods,
Emanating the great elements from his body,
Lifting up and hurling mountains,
And abiding in deathless life.
Like the pinnacle ornament at the top of a victory banner,
That very Dharma of pure vision
Did not decline for aeons.

Likewise, make your entry pure.
Through seeing, this Dharma is wholly purified,
And having found the eye of the lamp of wisdom,
Those who possess the unsurpassed essence
Find the path and enter on the path.
And what is that like?
A brahmin called Kunri,
Possessing the eye of Dharma, saw the meaning
And practiced according to the scripture of the great secret.
A brahmin called Kaini
Followed after the meaning with faith.
Seeing the gathering of the assembly of the great secret,
He came to possess the eye of Dharma
And came to see the essence of the meaning.
He attained the very stage of a great vidyādhara,
Perfect in spontaneously present great power.
Likewise, make it pure through seeing.
Others who here, toward those who actually practice,
Turn with a joyful mind
Are just like those who practice,
And in the result there is no higher or lower.
And what is that like?
To a yogin called
King Radiant Īśvara,
With a retinue of ten groups,
Who had entered on the aim of vidyādhara conduct,
The great fruit of mantra practice came,
And he gained many wondrous miracles,
So that he became renowned everywhere.
Then a brahmin of another school
Called Virtuous Disciple,
Having given rise to a most pure mind,
Set out to enter and to train,
But the king did not permit it and closed the secret door.
The brahmin, his faith unshaken,
Offered all the wealth and articles he had
To the king's retinue,
And, turning his mind toward
The land where the king dwelt, made aspirations;
He made aspirations single-pointedly.
Through the merit of holding in mind
Sublime rejoicing,
The king who practiced
And the brahmin who did not practice
Both obtained the result, with no higher or lower.
This is effortless and of great meaning;
It is the vidyādhara skilled in means.
Likewise, by rejoicing with joy
In others who practice,
The one who practices virtue and the one who does not
Both come to obtain an equal result.
The inconceivable, unfathomable
Victors train in this and know this,
And so have become friends to the beings of the three worlds
And lords of all.
And what is that like?
The brahmin Luminous Gift of old
Trained in the meaning of this great secret,
And when he made his own mind familiar with it,
He saw the meaning of the truth of mind itself
And became learned in all phenomena.
Since mind is the root of all,
From mind arise countless hundreds of thousands of phenomena.
Becoming a king of Dharma there,
He became renowned throughout all buddha fields
As the Buddha called the Lion of Speech.
Likewise, make your training pure.
The merit of a single great feast gathering
Is such that spreading great accumulations of giving
To countless śrāvakas, lifetime after lifetime,
Does not equal even a hundred-thousandth part of it.
Why is that? Because by pleasing the one,
All the victors are pleased.
The victors who pervade space
And the victor who appears as one alone
Are not separate; they are not different.
Their body, speech and mind are nothing but one.
It is only by the particular perspective of those to be tamed
That inconceivable displays are shown;
Hence the differences appear in that way.
And what is that like?
Two brahmins of pure deeds,
Called Virtuous and Luminous,
In each of whom joyful faith had arisen,
Made a pact with one another.
One made extensive offerings and gifts
To countless śrāvakas;
The other made an extensive feast offering.
Luminous, who practiced the feast,
Fully awakened in this single life.
Virtuous, who spread his giving,
Accomplished it in three countless aeons.
Therefore, those who practice in this place
Should practice correctly, like the yogin Luminous.
Whoever always upholds these
Will always obtain, in actual form,
Greatness and qualities just like those
Of Vajrasattva and the rest.
And what is that like?
The householder called Agnidatta,
Together with a hundred groups,
By practicing this constantly and without break,
Surely attained, in that very birth,
The likeness of the Buddha Vajrasattva
Together with his retinue.
Whoever, having realized the feast
That surely accomplishes for the sake of
Vajra body, speech and mind, speaks of it,
Through familiarizing, expressing and mentioning it,
Becomes a king of Dharma
And becomes the very crown of qualities.
And what is that like?
The sage and yogin Kalyana
Proclaimed the vast qualities of this feast
To sages of many kinds,
And from his definitive explanation of its meanings
Many sages attained the state of vidyādhara.
Kalyana, who taught them,
Became the Lord of Secrets, a king of Dharma.
So teach, explain or express
These secret meanings
To beings who are fit vessels.
By that one will obtain
Great qualities and my own confident eloquence.
Therefore the gathering of the Great Assembly,
The heart commitment of all the victors,
Is this secret feast of yoga.
Whatever yogin endowed with intelligence,
For an instant or a moment,
Fully sets out this feast of the victors
Fulfills the profound pledges
With all the victorious buddhas
And all the vidyādhara yogins,
And will obtain the eye of Dharma.
This has been the explanation of the accounts of former times
Concerning the qualities of practicing the feast
And the faults of practicing it wrongly.
Thus spoke Sattvavajra.

This is the seventh chapter, called Accounts of Past Times, from the great king of tantras, the Vajra Array, the Sūtra of the All-Gathering Awareness.

Then the Blessed One Sattvavajra gazed at the face of the Blessed One, the Lord of the Supreme Secret, with great expectation, in the manner of one awaiting a command, and, majestic in every way, remained in readiness at the point of being urged on by the retinue, and fell silent. Then, knowing that most of the retinue were urging him, they struck the ground with their yogic hands and whole bodies, let out the sound "a la la ho," and supplicated with one voice:

How wonderful! Great Vajrasattva,
Blazing with the wisdom that knows self-awareness,
Protector whose qualities pass beyond thought,
Victorious one, to you we bow.
How wonderful! Self-appearing, indivisible body,
Adorned with the major marks of pure merit,
The minor marks arrayed like grain, never tiring to behold,
To the supreme of the five bodies we bow.
How wonderful! Pure expanse of space,
Single speech that knows the various sounds as awareness,
Vajra speech beyond thought and beyond expression,
To the supreme of the five speeches we bow.
How wonderful! Secret mind like space,
Equality unmoving, yet each thing distinctly clear,
Essence of all-knowing awareness, supreme wisdom,
To the supreme of the five minds we bow.
How wonderful! Doing nothing, yet doing great deeds,
Unattached, free of attachment, yet a treasury of desire,
Great action that liberates all,
To supreme enlightened activity we bow.

Regent, we supplicate you:
Through the blessing of the compassion of the protector Maitreya,
Array the armored ranks of emanations in equal troops,
And, against the great hunting drive of saṃsāra,
In order utterly to slay suffering,
Perform the great act of blessing
Everything into a great charnel ground.
A ho!

When they had supplicated thus, that great Sattvavajra made the salutation of a yogin and asked the Blessed One, the Lord of the Supreme Secret: "A la la ho! Supreme of great bliss, king of all secrets, embodiment of the unsurpassed utmost secret, this most daring request, the wondrous words of the gathering of the Great Assembly, is truly marvelous. Considering them with a view to taming them, please satisfy their intentions just as they request."

When he had made this supplication, the Blessed One, the Lord of the Supreme Secret, knowing that the time for taming all had come, surrounded by the retinue of the wondrous secret command, entered into equipoise in the samādhi called Wondrous Display of the Charnel-Ground Ornaments. Having entered equipoise, he spoke these words that bless the retinue: hūṃ he he phaṭ hūṃ hūṃ viśva vajra krodha jvala maṇḍala ā ra li a li u li ta pa li traṃ stvaṃ kha li ho na śa staṃ bha ya hūṃ bruṃ phu ca khā hi phaṭ phaṭ ha la hūṃ

All the boundless ten directions of the six worlds became the maṇḍala of the charnel ground, and they shook, shook violently, and were utterly scattered.

Then the Blessed One, the Lord of the Supreme Secret, taught the heart of Sattvavajra a Dharma of the body that the rest of the retinue could not fathom. Transforming his whole body into the great lord of the charnel ground, he displayed the eight charnel grounds, the ten places of Heruka, the eight glorious attires, the nine great charnel-ground ornaments, the eighteen great signs of meaning, and the various enjoyments of the charnel ground, and gazed with the radiance of a smiling face. From this Sattvavajra understood. Realizing that by this great and mighty secret command the meaning had been explained, and that it was to be proclaimed in words, he spoke to the gathering of the assembly in words of deeply hidden intention:

"This great and wondrous secret command
Is concealed, and so profound and hard to realize.
Not knowing how to draw out the meaning from what it indicates,
Those of utterly childish and feeble intellect
Who think, 'Afflictions may be indulged at will,'
Are blind in the eye of definitive wisdom.
With little strength and force in the meaning,
Thinking to create the enjoyments of joy and bliss,
Though they practice and pursue signs at will,
Their own nature will not be accomplished.
The continuum ruined, the fruit scorched,
The sprout destroyed, how could anything arise?
For that reason, the Lord of Secrets, the king,
Declares everywhere that these meanings
Are not to be taught to those of feeble strength.
Do you all not know this?"

When he had spoken at length in this way, the gathering of the assembly supplicated him again: "How wonderful! Great being, will you speak such great words of the cause here within this sublime maṇḍala of the retinue? Or will you not practice them? Surely that cannot be. What will you accept and what reject? How wonderful! Who here is not a vessel? Since all possess the essence of awakening, release it, release it completely! Speak it out fully! You have no hindrance; speak without holding back!

Why do you not speak, unobstructed son?
Son of the Victors, this is the time.
This is the vessel for the secret essence.
This is the place of the maṇḍala of the secret command.
This is the way of the secret charnel ground.
You of compassion and wrath, we bow to you.
Secret and utmost secret, we bow to you.
Secret and primordially secret, we bow to you.
King of the maṇḍala, we bow to you.
Essence of the wheel, we bow to you.
What is the manner of the great secret essence?
What is the method of the maṇḍala of the secret command?
What is the meaning of the result, bliss?
For the yogins of the future,
What is the method by which, when they practice,
They will come to know the attainment they seek?
King of the Great Elements, please explain!"

When they had supplicated thus, the sublime Sattvavajra laughed broadly with a great laugh, and as he let out the peals of laughter "a a he he," the whole retinue too let out a great sound of laughter. From the rays of his delight, the glory of wisdom came forth as various joyful music, ringing and resounding. Then the sublime Sattvavajra drew his whole body upright, donned the various charnel-ground attires, made the eighteen signs blaze, and with majestic speech explained this ocean of secret mantra, and these great secret maṇḍalas, the display of signs, the accomplishment of the meaning in its various enumerations:

"Yogins who rely on the meaning
That is singly, truly and definitively accomplished,
And who wish to accomplish it without obstruction,
Just as launderers
Cleanse white cloth so that it takes the dye,
Among the activities of yoga, first of all
Cherish the rites of fulfillment and purification.
Before entering the profound,
In order to cut off at the very depth of mind itself,
Do not keep company in anything at all
With people of the Lesser Vehicle."

Articles that have come from them,
And whatever has come upon the turtle's head,
With a great and deeply stirred mind
Cast them into the ocean and abandon them.
If, through the force of a person,
The penalty of a breach has come about,
Bring down the water of the nature of reality on all, up to the protectors,
And perform extensively the rite of fulfillment and averting.
Before beginning the practice, early or earlier still,
Some number of days beforehand,
Purify wrongdoing, give rise to the mind of awakening,
And set your aspiration on the aim of virtue.
Beggars, those fond of begging,
And all who make their living by begging,
Arrange them in groups, sections and rows,
And make them the field for the manure of compassion.
Medicines, grains and essences,
And whatever has become the finest of nourishment,
Ground, or mixed into flour,
Cast into rivers, streams and great lakes,
And give them away without attachment.
Taking on virtuous patrons,
Establish places for healing the sick, for learning, for practice and for meditation,
And show them supreme honor;
And make the great offerings of the wheel of the seasons.
Relying on all the profound volumes
That proclaim the ocean of the sounds of speech,
Through recitation, service and honor,
Perform vast and extensive deeds.
In order to perfect the accumulations of primordial wisdom,
Give the supreme gifts of the accumulation of wisdom,
Teachings, or volumes and the like,
To those endowed with supreme intellect.
In order to purify the ground of all
Through meditation and familiarization,
Exercise that power of supreme intellect and mind
In many tens of millions of samādhis.
Turning the wheel of Dharma well,
Look to the higher wisdom that purifies the continuum.
At toll stations and on toll roads
Remove all harms of hardship,
Turn the wheel of immeasurable compassion,
And do not interrupt the wheel of giving.
In water or in earth or in the sky,
In fire, and likewise in the winds,
Make into a wheel the great work
Of multiplying and offering the remains and relics.
To avert the deeds of immediate retribution,
Grind them to dust in the continuum of body and mind,
And in the palace of cloud masses of the noble ones,
With bodies as numerous as atoms, make offerings and confess with your mind.
Do the deeds that please the Victors.
From the great works of the union of great means,
Perform the supreme fire rite of four kinds;
That is what averts the deeds of immediate retribution.
The wheels from which compassion arises:
Strive at them in whatever way you are accustomed.
To the hosts who guard the teaching
Perform acts of increasing gifts and offerings.
Among such accumulations of virtue as these,
Abandon all that does not serve the aim,
And raise the great army of the accumulations of diligence.
These great works, heaps of merit and the rest,
Are not produced for any other purpose:
They are to make the single-pointed yogin who holds to the meaning
One possessed of continuum and fit to be a vessel.
And if, being of feeble strength, one has not succeeded,
Gather all the accumulated virtue into great heaps,
Reckon up the meaning of the accumulations of deeds,
Draw out the force of longing and shake out your strength,
And, like the sun rising, a river flowing, the moon reflected,
Cherish its being accomplished by the force of its own nature.
Therefore, even the subtlest thing upward
Should be made to serve one's own aim.
The yogin of subtlety who practices
What is to be practiced in this way, his intelligence increasing,
Having exhausted the small and the smaller to the very end,
Should, by the wheel of the yoga of fulfillment,
Perform this act of fulfilling, restoring and reinstating
Whatever is ruined, torn, broken or mixed.
By the special character of the great work of fulfillment,
There is no fixed rule for examining the site:
A great and wholly fresh site,
Where what is ugly, faulty and inauspicious
Has been removed and is no longer present,
Becomes the place for fulfillment.
Having found such a delightful place,
Invite a teacher who has the warmth of realization,
And honor him with respectful service, placing the royal teaching on your crown.
Then, as the specific requests,
On a maṇḍala plate merely sprinkled with water,
Made like the heart of a mirror,
The offerings of the five pleasing kinds,
Their substances made from pure ingredients,
Adorned with various yogas of conditions,
Cloud masses heaped high,
Are offered to the Victor so that he is pleased and the breach fulfilled.
To the protectors who guard the outer circle,
With the five sense pleasures and the like,
Wish-fulfilling enjoyments together with their conditions,
Make offerings from the portion of qualities, entrusting them with your prayers;
Pleased, they become allies of fulfillment.
To the objects of compassion in the three realms,
If, from great accumulations of gifts of articles,
One satisfies their wishes,
Then, being pleased, they become allies of fulfillment.
Even the classes of obstructors who have become obstructors
Are not to be shut out of mind, through the meaning of equality.
If specifically, with various foods,
The obstructors are satisfied, then all of them,
Through the meaning of equality, appear
As causes of fulfillment, if only in a small way.
Thus, as for what is to be fulfilled
By these acts of fulfillment,
It appears in five kinds:
The great breach of everything,
The breach of the root, the breach of the branches,
Likewise the breach of recitation and the incidental breach: these five.
The means of fulfillment are also five:
Fulfillment through the deeds of conduct,
Through substances, through meaning, through samādhi,
And the great secret means of the pith instructions.
Each of these is also fivefold.
For fulfillment by way of conduct,
I shall explain five kinds.
For the first, five hundred and thirty thousand;
For a root breach, fifty-three thousand.
For a breach of the branches,
Complete five thousand three hundred.
For a breach of recitation, five hundred and thirty.

For an incidental breach,
Fulfill it with fifteen, one and three.
The supreme means of fulfillment through substances is this.
If, through a lapse in some guarding,
The pledge of awakening has been torn,
Fulfill the pledge of the delusion family.
Invite a teacher of the commoner caste,
And confess your faults and transgressions a thousand times, without concealment.
With great substances such as gold,
Ransom with that wealth whatever pledge was lost.
Give rise to the mind of awakening, purify your continuum, and listen to the teachings.
If the vajra pledge has been torn,
Fulfill the pledge of the aversion family.
Invite a teacher of the merchant caste,
And confess your misdeeds a hundred thousand times, without concealment.
With great substances such as crystal,
Ransom with that wealth whatever pledge was lost.
Strive to cultivate meditative concentration and samādhi.
If the jewel pledge has been breached,
Fulfill the pledge of the pride family.
Invite a teacher of the royal caste,
And confess your misdeeds eighty thousand times, without concealment.
With great substances such as beryl,
Ransom with that wealth whatever pledge was lost.
Multiply the bodily remains and relics.
If the lotus pledge has been torn,
Fulfill the pledge of the desire family.
Invite a teacher of the caṇḍāla caste,
And confess your misdeeds a hundred times, without concealment.
With great substances such as coral,
Ransom with that wealth whatever pledge was lost.
Recite the profound volumes that are to be explained.
If the action pledge has been torn,
Fulfill the pledge of the jealousy family.
Invite a spiritual friend of the brahmin caste,
And confess your misdeeds fifteen times, without concealment.
With substances such as sapphire,
Ransom with that wealth whatever pledge was lost.
Perform the great deeds of offering, giving and merit.
By this the great act of fulfillment is accomplished.

The supreme means of fulfillment through meaning
I shall also explain in five ways.
Having meditated on all phenomena as space,
Settle in your mind all habitual tendencies, good and bad,
As nothing whatsoever.
When you have grown familiar with that, there is no fault.
From a teacher learned in everything,
Hear the vast and extensive Dharma,
And perform the deeds of reflection extensively.
Through such deeds held in the mind,
A root breach comes to be fulfilled.
If the branches have been breached,
Examine all the profound volumes
That teach the meaning of the nature of reality as space,
And examine their meanings extensively.
By that there is no breach of the branches.
If the pledge of recitation has been breached,
For a teacher, a learned person,
Take on the role of patron
For a teaching that establishes a great scripture,
Gather all who have the power of intellect,
And proclaim the teaching, the nectar of the sublime Dharma.
If there has been an incidental breach,
Write out the vast volumes of the teaching
And give them as a gift of Dharma.
By that the incidental breach comes to be fulfilled.

The supreme means through the power of samādhi is this.
At the crown of the one bound by habitual tendencies,
A light from the red channel, the size of a mirror,
Blazes intensely, gleaming and clear,
And burns the aggregates bound up with attachment.
From the oneness of the nature of the liberated mind
And the nature of light,
It becomes three spheres of light,
White, red and blue.
Emanating as white, its pervasion is great;
Emanating as red, its passion is great;
Blue is the seed of the mind.
From the three gathered into one,
The habitual tendencies of body, speech and mind are burned,
And the pledges of body, speech and mind are fulfilled.
From the white maṇḍala of the wind moon,
A red blowing wind arises,
And scatters the habitual tendencies of signs.
From the red maṇḍala of the water moon,
Blue water of primordial wisdom arises,
And washes away all the grime of things.
When it has become clear in this way,
The yogin speaks these words:
"To burn substances, fire is needed,
But to burn habitual tendencies, no fire is needed:
The fire of the primordial wisdom of awareness arises
And burns the firewood of signs.
To scatter the world, wind is needed,
But to scatter signs, no wind is needed:
The wind of the primordial wisdom of awareness arises
And sweeps away the masses of the dust of signs.
To wash the body, water is needed,
But to wash the mind, no water is needed:
In the river of equality, the bathing pool,
A stream of the river of primordial wisdom arises
And washes away all the grime of habitual tendencies.
oṃ krodhi śvari sarva pāpaṃ śodhaya oṃ āḥ hūṃ raṃ yaṃ khaṃ"
By speaking these words,
The penalty of a breach does not arise.

As for the great act of fulfilling all together:
Gather the substances and articles of the families
At the site and bless them,
Make the outer and inner boundaries firm,
And for whatever has contravened
The pledges of each family,
With offering, confession, and remorseful honor,
Adopt the eightfold discipline,
Purify the place of meditation and prepare the substances.
As for the pledge of the delusion family,
Which is to be understood and realized:
If, through the power of perverse ignorance,
The fierce pledge has lapsed,
In the palace, the emblem of space,
Abiding at the center of a moon maṇḍala,
Is the nature of A, as clear light.
When its radiance stirs and becomes agitated,
By striking it forcefully with PHAṬ,
The light gathers in and the sky of the aeon clears,
The darkness of the mountain of delusion is dispelled,
And the sprout of awakening blazes forth.
When it has become clear in this way,
The yogin speaks these words:
"How wonderful! From the pledge of the Sugatas,
The mind of awakening, blissful, clear and blazing,
Was not given up for the aim, and its ground was produced;
Yet from the continuum of the great lineage of the buddhas,
As the moon is hidden by a mountain,
From a nature that arises from causes,
The sprout of awakening has all but set.
In the buddha field of the aggregate of form,
Supreme bliss, the great expanse equal to space, is clear.
Through the great means that conquers by its antidote,
I fulfill it! I fulfill the heart pledge of the aeon!
With this supreme secret substance of meaning,
May the heart pledges of the aeon of awakening
Be fulfilled, and be purified for the sake of the intention.
sarva tathāgata puta samaya ho"
Having spoken these words,
Join your hands in a mudrā at the crown in the manner of a sign.
As for the pledge of the aversion family:
In the palace, the emblem of consciousness,
Abiding at the center of a vajra vase,
The nature of HŪṂ blazes as clear light.
When its radiance stirs and becomes agitated,
By striking it forcefully with PHAṬ,
The light gathers in, and by the vajra diamond
The mountain of aversion is destroyed,
And the mirror of wisdom blazes as clear light.
When it has become clear in this way,
The yogin speaks these words:
"How wonderful! The vajra pledges,
Great mind, primordial wisdom clear and blazing:
From the continuum of the great lineage of the supreme mind,
As a sword's edge is blunted,
From a nature derived from that,
The mirror of wisdom has all but set.
In the buddha field of the aggregate of consciousness,
Spheres of clear and blazing primordial wisdom radiate.
Through the great means that conquers by its antidote,
I fulfill it! I fulfill the heart pledge of the aeon!
With this supreme secret substance of meaning,
I fulfill the heart pledge of the aeon of the supreme mind.
May it be fulfilled, and be purified for the sake of the intention.
oṃ sarva tathāgata samaya ho"

Having spoken these words,
Join your hands in a mudrā in the manner of the sign at the heart.
As for the pledge of the pride family,
Which is to be taken up after making all equal:
If, not having overcome that perverse pride,
The fierce pledge has lapsed,
In the palace, at the center of the emblem that is the source of birth,
Abiding at the center of a blazing jewel,
The nature of sva blazes as clear light.
When its radiance stirs and becomes agitated,
By striking it forcefully with phaṭ,
The light gathers in, and by the jewel wheel
The great boulder of pride is destroyed,
And the flowers of the tree of merit blaze forth.
When it has become clear in this way,
The yogin speaks these words:
"How wonderful! From the jewel pledge,
Merit and qualities blazing with light
Were not given up for the aim, and their ground was produced;
Yet from the continuum equal to the great lineage of the supreme cause,
As clouds gather thick across the sky,
By a nature derived from causes,
The beautiful light of the flowers has all but faded.
In the buddha field of the aggregate of feeling,
The wisdom of greatness blazes in equality.
Through the great means that conquers by its antidote,
I fulfill it! I fulfill the heart pledge of the aeon!
With this supreme secret substance of meaning,
I fulfill the heart pledge of the aeon of the supreme body.
May it be fulfilled, and be purified for the sake of the intention.
oṃ sarva tathāgata ratna samaya ho"
Having spoken these words,
Join your hands in a mudrā in the manner of the sign at the forehead.
As for the pledge of the desire family,
Which is to be practiced after realization:
If, unsated in the ocean of perverse desire,
The fierce pledge has lapsed,
In the palace, at the center of the lotus emblem,
Abiding at the center of a blazing lotus,
The nature of oṃ blazes as clear light.
When its radiance stirs and becomes agitated,
By striking it forcefully with phaṭ,
The light gathers in, and by the weapon of the lotus
The ocean of desire is dried up,
And the primordial wisdom of the lotus Dharma blazes forth.
When it has become clear in this way,
The yogin speaks these words:
"How wonderful! From the lotus pledge,
The primordial wisdom of the lotus blazing with light
Was not given up for the aim, and its ground was produced;
Yet from the continuum equal to the great lineage of the supreme speech,
Like the thorns among a thousand-petaled lotus,
By a nature derived from causes,
The primordial wisdom of the lotus has all but set.
In the buddha field of the aggregate of perception,
The light of the realized nature of reality radiates.
Through the great means that conquers by its antidote,
I fulfill it! I fulfill the heart pledge of the aeon!
With this supreme secret substance of meaning,
May the heart pledges of the aeon of the supreme speech
Be fulfilled, and be purified for the sake of the intention.
oṃ sarva tathāgata dharma samaya ho"
Having spoken these words,
Join your hands in a mudrā in the manner of the sign at the tongue.
As for the pledge of the jealousy family,
Which is to be practiced after realization:
If, through the power of perverse grasping of jealousy,
The fierce pledge has lapsed,
In the palace, at the center of the secret emblem,
Abiding at the center of a vajra sword,
The nature of ha blazes as clear light.
When its radiance stirs and becomes agitated,
By striking it forcefully with phaṭ,
The light gathers in, and by the vajra sword
The weapons of jealousy are blunted,
And the primordial wisdom of the vajra sword blazes forth.
When it has become clear in this way,
The yogin speaks these words:
"How wonderful! From the pledge of enlightened activity,
The vajra sword blazing with light
Was not given up for the aim, and its ground was produced;
Yet from the continuum equal to the great lineage of enlightened activity,
As the clear sun is obscured by clouds,
By a nature derived from causes,
The great vajra light has all but set.
In the buddha field of the aggregate of formations,
The light of the weapon of primordial wisdom radiates.
Through the great means that conquers by its antidote,
I fulfill it! I fulfill the heart pledge of the aeon!
With this supreme secret substance of meaning,
May the heart pledges of the aeon of enlightened activity
Be fulfilled, and be purified for the sake of the intention.
oṃ sarva tathāgata karma samaya ho"
Having spoken these words,
Join your hands in a mudrā in the manner of the sign of the body.
By this too the great act of fulfillment is accomplished.

The supreme meaning of the means of pith instruction is this.
For whichever of the root and branch pledges
Has been breached,
Fulfill it by the five supreme means of pith instruction.
If one has fallen into a great breach,
Those persons who have become teachers,
In the sight of nine kings,
Should undertake nine great practices
And carry out the discipline of the nine great supreme ones,
And whoever is one's own consort
Should be offered as consort to nine teachers.
Likewise, for breaches of the root, the branches,
Recitation, and incidental breaches,
If the special practices of meaning,
Seven, or five, or three, or one,
Are applied in their order,
That becomes the supreme fulfillment.

The objects of fulfillment are also of five kinds.
All the Sugatas and Victors,
When summarized, are of five kinds:
Delusion, aversion, pride,
Desire and jealousy, these five.
Because they are what has become the root,
And the ground too is entirely transformed by them,
Know that these are the cause
Of pledges breached and unbreached,
And of all enjoyments, all rejecting and accepting.
By the power of designating the cause itself as the cause,
Like poison that, transformed, becomes a great medicine,
Transform that very thing into a powerful pledge.
By the particular force of taming,
