They likewise gather into the five families:
Vajra, jewel, lotus, action, and the rest.
Their supreme heart pledges, too,
Are taught to gather into five.
If, having realized them and vowed to guard and practice them,
One violates and breaks each of them,
Each must be restored individually.
Breaks of body are to be set back in place.
Violations and errors of speech are to be restored.
For violations and errors of mind there is no repair.
For the pledge of the vajra heart,
The great signs that fulfilling has brought fulfillment
Are these:
The relics called śarīram,
Which arise from the great lineages
Of the victorious buddhas and the vidyādharas,
Show everything as omens.
Placed with purity inside a crystal casket and kept there,
When the penalty of a breach has arisen,
Their color turns black and they vanish.
If the breach is fulfilled or restored,
Or if the sacred pledge has not been breached,
Their color becomes white and gleaming.
When good qualities arise,
They multiply greatly in number.
If they are black or unclear,
Hasten, concentrate, and perform the rites of circling and averting.
So, without incurring a breach,
When restoring them,
Rely on this great sign.
When, examining these with great care,
They have become white and gleaming,
That is the sign that breaches of root and branches
Have been fulfilled and restored.

Therefore, as the chief of the yogic activities,
Perform the rites of purification and of fulfillment.

When the Blessed One Sattvavajra had spoken thus, among those gathered in that assembly were the five Great Gods (Śiva and his like), lords of the five afflictions, and others, who from the hosts of Māra had come into the family of the gods, had become the retinue of the Buddha, holding the noble family, and were seated there, blessed so as to be fit vessels for the teaching. Of these, the one called Vajra Skull-Bearing Brahmin, chief of all desire, and the others addressed him thus: "If one does not act according to the Blessed One's command, what will be the birthplace of punishment, and what the karmic maturation, for those who have become faithless, irreverent, contrary and mistaken? Please explain this to us at length."

When they had asked this, the Blessed One Sattvavajra declared: "Lord of Desire and the rest, you ask well about the birthplace of punishment for breaching the sacred pledge. I shall explain what will satisfy your minds; listen!

If any of these roots and branches
Should be destroyed or breached:
At the very beginning of yogic activity,
When one entered the secret door of scripture,
The master who possesses the meaning
Consecrated the vajra oath,
And the pledge water placed upon the tongue
Was sworn with a curse of the Vajra Hell.
That water, having become the letter hūṃ,
Dissolves into the center of the heart,
And there, becoming the great blood-drinker,
The wrathful one called Rakṣa,
An iron scorpion with nine heads,
It sucks out and drinks the heart's blood,
Slays the breaker in great suffering,
And he falls into the Vajra Hell.
If the oath has not been breached,
What was placed upon the tongue is argha;
It dissolves into the center of the heart,
Becomes a wisdom being one finger-span in size,
Purifies every obscuration,
And causes one to be born as a child of the buddhas.
If the pledge of desire is breached,
One goes to the great city of the womb.
If the pledge of aversion is breached,
One goes into the race of rakṣa rākṣasas.
If the pledge of delusion is breached,
One falls into the body of a great, dull-witted pig
That eats filth for its food.
If the pledge of pride is breached,
One falls into the body of a black tortoise.
If the pledge of envy is breached,
One falls into the body of a nine-headed sea monster (makara).
If all the great pledges are breached,
One falls into the body of black Rudra.
Unless one meets with the condition of the rite,
There is no possibility of liberation."

Thus spoke Sattvavajra.

The vidyādharas, lords of the five afflictions, let out a very great wail, and at that time they said: "Oh! Blessed One, Blessed One! According to what the Blessed One has said, we have causes for a fall. At first, when we were bound by the Blessed One's command, lacking faith and respect for the command, we disparaged the command and mocked it; we reviled and mocked the Teacher and our companions; and so on. Since we committed many causes of downfall, it was certain that we would go to the birthplace of punishment. But since the Blessed One's great compassion has taken hold of us and placed us in the retinue of the teaching, we have, it seems, attained liberation. Even so, under the power of intense remorse, we confess the obscurations of mind through the mind of awakening; we confess the obscurations of speech by uttering them in words and crying out; we confess the obscurations of body by offering our bodies as seat and throne. If, then, we beg to be held by the Blessed One's compassion, what is the antidote? By what method is confession made? To what object is it confessed? What is its cause? Blessed One, please explain. Sugata, please protect us with your compassion."

The Blessed One declared: "Great vidyādharas who bear human skulls, listen! It is excellent that, feeling remorse for the occasions of your transgression, you have asked this. What is the antidote to transgression? If one wishes to confess misdeeds, it is to give rise to intense remorse, so that the hairs of the body stand on end and tears are shed. The method is of four kinds. What are they? To know, through the continuum of great wisdom, that all habitual tendencies are without intrinsic nature; to burn all habitual tendencies with the fire of great meditative absorption, so that they are no more; to dispel all the darkness of habitual tendencies with the light rays of the mind of awakening, so that they are purified; and to wash them with the water of vast conduct. The objects before whom confession is to be made are these: the buddhas, the vidyādharas, the deities, the masters, the scriptures, one's companions, and whatever serves as a sacred support. The cause for which confession is to be made is this: the confession of obscurations great and small; that is, the confession of the coarse afflictions that manifestly arise, and the confession of the latent tendencies and of the obscurations to the knowable. Therefore, attend to the actions that have become transgressions with intense remorse, examine them with skill in means, distinguish the obscurations great and small part by part, and confess them with reverence!"

When he had declared this, the great vidyādharas who bear human skulls were pleased and delighted, and, having made the prostration of yogic union, they sang this song of remorse and anguish:

oṃ The maṇḍala that is the nature of the supreme body of wisdom,
Like the full moon, is free of all elaboration,
Yet, deity who makes all equal, like the clear light of the sun of compassion,
Sugatas, please remain, and turn your minds to me.
Ineffable wisdom, unmoving, supreme expanse of Dharma;
Great bliss, Body of Perfect Enjoyment, chief of the five families;
Hosts of miraculous emanations, the vast display
Of compassion and skillful means: to you I prostrate.

With unsurpassed Samantabhadra clouds of offerings,
Both actually present and emanated by the mind,
Filling completely the vast and pure expanse of space,
We make vast outer, inner and secret offerings.
In the bhaga of the mother Samantabhadrī,
In the state where there is no meeting and no parting,
May you be pleased by the nondual awakening mind of a single taste!
The nature of mind is the dharmadhātu, the great space;
Since the nature of reality is pure, vast and luminous,
Since the expanse of yoga itself transcends speech and thought,
To equality, the awakening mind, I bow always.
In Samantabhadra, the Great Perfection, all-pervading from the very beginning,
The array of the outer, inner and secret maṇḍalas
Is appearance and existence wholly pure, the expanse of the mother Samantabhadrī;
The elements and what derives from them, spontaneously perfect as father and mother;
The great secret, fully perfect, she whose very nature is the great dhāraṇī;
Luminous as the nondual great bindu;
Appearing as anything at all, the unchanging deities of great bliss.
The secret maṇḍala, free of meeting and parting:
The Great Being, the Blessed One, father and mother,
The male and female bodhisattvas, the hosts of wrathful males and females,
The vajra goddesses, the lords of the hosts and masters of the gatherings,
Those who love like mothers and cherish like sisters, and all the rest,
The women who watch over good and ill and follow up the pledges,
The vajra oath-bound ones who stand as witnesses: please turn your minds to us!
We, the vajra skull-bearers, heirs through compassion,
All the lords of the haughty ones gathered here,
Have given rise to the awakening mind for the sake of beings,
In order to attain the unsurpassed state.
The discipline of each of the ocean of teachings,
The supreme vows of body, speech, mind and union,
The vajra pledges, hard to transgress, a lasting charge,
And the pledges, general and especially particular:
Earlier and later we received them and swore to keep them in our charge.
Although we had no thought of straying from the meaning or turning back,
Through the laziness that thinks, "There is still time to do it later,"
And through strength and vigor grown slack and feeble,
Lacking vigilance, we came to contravene the pledges.
At first, when the oath was bound upon us by the command,
We were irreverent toward the command, went against it, and mocked it.
In the middle, when the great charge of the training fell to us,
We did not strive in the training and were careless of the meaning.
At last, when the fruit was to be accomplished in this life,
We could not follow the command and the texts, and went against the meaning.
Obscurations great and small, noticed and unnoticed,
Through the power of an ignorance that knows nothing at all,
We have gone against the Teacher's command and the pledges:
Hero, in your compassion, please forgive me!
From the beginningless reaches of time in saṃsāra,
By the force of karma we have wandered long.
Having drunk the poison of afflictions and wrong views,
We are sorely tormented by the disease of suffering.
The compassion of the Sugatas, the king of physicians,
Bestowing the nectar of the pledges, the medicine of liberation,
Pacifies the diseases of affliction and delusion,
And sustains us to the heart of unsurpassed awakening.
In the loving protector, lord of compassion, I take refuge.

Having said this, they cried out a la la ho, relied intensely on the antidote, and offered their bodies as seat and throne. Having been thoroughly purified in the sense of bowing down through the awakening mind, they stripped their bodies stark naked. Because they made confession with intense shame and remorse, the Sugatas were pleased. Thereby their obscurations were purified, and as a sign that the accumulation of merit was complete, their bodies were parted from their clothing, and the skulls they bore became like the moon on the eighth day. As a sign that they had attained the grounds, they came to abide as the support beneath the feet of the Tathāgata.

Then Sattvavajra, delighted, declared: "Skull-bearers, it is excellent that you have confessed through the act of remorse. The obscurations of affliction have been purified. Now, in order to purify the obscurations to the knowable and to complete the accumulation of wisdom, listen with your faculty of hearing to the sounds of the secret mantra in the speech of the buddhas. Through that you will leap from ground to ground, and the fruit will be brought to complete perfection." When he had said this, the minds of the retinue were gladdened.

This concludes the eighth chapter, on the way of accomplishing the rite of fulfillment, from the great king of tantras, the Vajra Array, the Sūtra of the All-Gathering Awareness.

Then the Blessed One, the Supreme Secret himself, entered into the meditative absorption called Expansive Magical Display. The sattva Vajragarbha also entered into the meditative absorption called Magical Display. The assembled retinue, too, entered into the meditative absorption called Various Magical Displays. Then the great assembled retinue saw all phenomena as being nothing whatsoever. When all phenomena had become clear as nothing whatsoever, from the space of the empty nature of reality this sound came forth, like an echo:

"Not abiding, not conceiving, free of thought;
Without attachment, free of attachment, beyond all objects;
Nothing at all arises; it is not anything;
Self-arisen, self-pacified, free of expression."

As this sound came forth, the Teacher drew out the greatness of the meaning, and the retinue trained their skill. All were absorbed deep in the meaning and saw the space of the nature of reality.

Then the Blessed One, the Lord of the Supreme Secret himself, entered into the meditative absorption called Stream of Compassion. Then, in the space of the nature of reality, there dawned a disk of the sun, the reflection of compassion, the awakening mind. Then this true understanding arose in the great assembled retinue: "Ah! Although the nature of all phenomena is thus, beings who do not know it so are sinking into the ocean of saṃsāra; how pitiful this is!" And so the awakening mind of compassion arose in them.

Then from the light rays at the heart of the sun a miraculous sound came forth:

"The light of the sun that pervades everything
Is compassion, the awakening mind.
It shines, dispelling the darkness of conceptual thought;
It is the blazing wish-fulfilling jewel."

As this sound came forth, all phenomena became clear as the light rays of the awakening mind.

Then again the Blessed One, the Lord of the Supreme Secret himself, entered into the meditative absorption called Cause of the Arising of Miracles. Then the great assembled retinue saw the white letter a, the cause of all phenomena, dawn from the heart of the sun. Then from the a a miraculous sound came forth:

"a is the treasury of letters, free of attachment,
The cause of every miraculous conception;
Free of the faults of affliction, the color of the moon,
It is the cause from which various miracles arise."

As this sound came forth, the assembled hosts saw all phenomena abiding as the nature of miracle. Then the Blessed One, the Lord of the Supreme Secret, while doing nothing at all, appeared as the nature of various miracles.

Then the great mother called Ākāśadhātvīśvarī entered into the meditative absorption called Blessing All Phenomena as the Bhaga of Space. When she had entered it, the great assembled retinue saw all phenomena as having the nature of all-pervading space. Then blue light rays radiated from the heart of the mother's bhaga and stirred the a at the heart of the sun, whereupon a blue letter e radiated forth and remained hovering there, and from it this spontaneous miraculous sound came forth:

"a is the nature of e;
From a arises e, pervading space, spreading wide;
Unchanging, unobstructed, beyond thought,
Without edge or center, it spreads everywhere."

As this sound came forth, everything became evenly one as the nature of space.

Then again the great mother Karmadhātvīśvarī entered into the meditative absorption called the Light and Moving Maṇḍala of Wind. Thereby the great assembled retinue saw all phenomena abiding as the nature of wind. Then from that wind a miraculous sound came forth:

"From the wisdom blessed by a,
The miracle of wind dawned as yaṃ;
Possessing the strength that lifts up everything,
By nature moving, it drifts without obstruction."

As this sound came forth, it swirled into a maṇḍala of wind.

Then the mother called Dharmadhātvīśvarī entered into the meditative absorption of blazing, radiating fire. The great assembled retinue saw all phenomena abiding as the nature of fire. Then in the light rays of the fire a miraculous sound came forth:

"From the wisdom blessed by a,
The miracle dawned as the essence of fire;
Blazing and illuminating, wisdom radiates;
All things are consumed, and the expanse of fire whirls."

As this sound came forth, an eon of fire spread out without limit.

Then again the mother called Ratnadhātvīśvarī entered into the meditative absorption of the gathering ocean. Thereby the great assembled retinue saw all phenomena abiding as the nature of water. Then from the light rays of the waves this miraculous sound came forth:

"From the light rays of the causal letter,
The miracle of the essence of water dawned;
It is equal to the limits of space;
The winds and the oceans all spread wide."

As this sound came forth, everything abided pervaded by the nature of the mother Ratnadhātvīśvarī.

Then the great mother called Vajradhātvīśvarī entered into the meditative absorption called Blessing All Realms. Then the great assembled retinue saw all phenomena as having the nature of the grounds, fully perfected. Then from the ground called Blazing Vajra a miraculous sound came forth:

"From the nonreferential, as manifold
Appearance, this miracle has arisen.
The meaning of perfecting the ten grounds of yoga,
Being spontaneously present, is great bliss."

As this sound came forth, they came to understand the particulars of taming on the nine grounds.

Then the sattva Vajragarbha entered into the meditative absorption called Stacked Mountains. Thereby the great assembled retinue saw all phenomena as mountains of blazing vajras. Then from their light this miraculous sound came forth:

"From the space-expanse of pure wisdom
The mountain of blazing vajras has dawned;
Vast and deep, it towers to the heights of the sky,
And crushes the mountain of conceptual thought."

As this sound came forth, the mountain of blazing vajras overpowered all the mountains of affliction.

Then the five great Dhātvīśvarīs entered into the meditative absorption called the Pure Space-Expanse of the Mother, the Palace of Profound Wisdom. From the heart of the bhaga this miraculous sound came forth:

"From the light rays of the secret bhaga
Comes the palace where the Sugatas dwell,
Immeasurable like the limits of space,
The expanse of the ocean of Victors of the three times."

So the sound came forth. As it came forth, all of space became palaces without limit.

Then the Blessed One, the Supreme Secret himself, entered into the meditative absorption called All Phenomena Fully Complete in the Body, also called the meditative absorption Emanating All Mudrās. Then the great assembled retinue saw all the phenomena of saṃsāra and nirvāṇa complete in the Blessed One, the Lord of the Supreme Secret, from the seat and throne beneath his feet up to the crown protuberance on his head. Then again they saw the desire realm of saṃsāra complete as the ocean of blood of attachment, and the form realm of saṃsāra complete as the outspread ground of human corpses. They saw the formless realm complete in the skeletons. On the summit of Mount Meru, paved with pearls and filled with crags of blazing precious jewels, dense with the blazing light of emerald, they saw the śrāvakas, the pratyekabuddhas and the bodhisattvas complete. Above the precious jewels, on crags filled with three-pronged and five-pronged vajras, they saw those of Kriyā, Upaya and Yoga complete. Above the great vajra crag, on a maṇḍala of white lotus, they saw all the yogins of the generation stage complete. Above the lotus, where twenty-four suns and twenty-four moons were arranged in union, they saw the twenty-four male and twenty-four female Īśvaras complete. Above the male and female Īśvaras were the eight chief animals; and in order that the yoga of the great vidyādharas, having purified the afflictions, should purify the obscurations to the knowable, they saw all the vidyādharas, fully complete on the grounds, upon the brave tiger, the terrifying lion, the vicious leopard, the fierce bear, the elephant of strength, the majestic buffalo, the seizing sea monster, and the snake of aversion. In the great blazing palace they saw the mother Samantabhadrī complete. In the nine great masses of fire they saw the nine doors of wisdom, stacked one upon another, blazing completely. On the eight feet they saw the four secret mothers and the four gatekeepers complete. The eighteen great ornaments of the charnel ground shone clearly as the eighteen great mātṛkās, fully complete.

The forty-two hand emblems shine clearly as these forty-two: ten sorceresses brandishing staffs, holding in their hands ten blazing garuḍas, from whose beaks various weapons radiate; eight ḍākinīs brandishing staffs, holding in their hands eight lions with gaping jaws, from whose mouths eight blazing swords radiate; eight swift-goers brandishing staffs, holding in their hands eight tigers baring their fangs, from whose mouths various vajras radiate; eight mātṛkās brandishing staffs, holding in their hands eight human heads bound with vajra chains and whirled about by the brains; and eight blazing goddesses brandishing staffs, holding in their hands eight blazing masses of fire from which sparks radiate.

The forty-two hands shine clearly as these forty-two: the four blazing blood-drinkers, the nine male and nine female kiṅkaras, and the ten devouring fathers and ten slaying mothers. The twenty-one faces shine clearly as the twenty-one secret charnel grounds that devour existence. The sixty-four blazing eyes shine clearly as the sixty-four Herukas, fathers and mothers. The twenty-one heads shine clearly as the twenty-one buddhas of the three times.

From the crowns of the seven heads on the right, a buddha body emerges from each, and on each one's head a wheel blazes, together with light rays. From the seven heads in the center, a buddha head emerges from each as a head ornament, and on each one's body there shine seven blazing stūpas of precious jewels. From the seven heads on the left, a buddha body emerges from each as a head ornament, and on each one's head there shine seven precious jewels. From the hubs of these wheels, the vases of the stūpas, and the tips of the jewels blaze ten great banners: the iron banner of the sparrow; the deer banner of those to be tamed; the leopard banner of the beasts of prey; the vajra banner of emanation; the black-snake banner of the precious; the tiger banner of the hero; the human banner of splendor; the blood banner of brilliant red; the lion banner of great prowess; and the bear banner of great ferocity. The tips of these banners converge into one, a golden crown protuberance blazing with a thousand finials.

Then the great assembled retinue saw him fully complete from the seat and throne beneath his feet up to the crown protuberance on his head. They saw the yoga that is complete in the twenty-one buddhas emerging from the heads and in the ten great banners fully complete. They saw the thousand golden finials at the tip of the banner of the crown protuberance shining clearly as the very yoga of the Great Perfection. When they had seen this and it had become clear, the whole retinue was overwhelmed by the meaning of all phenomena being fully complete in the body, and wonder arose in them. The great yogins praised the body fully:

"How wonderful! Because all phenomena are fully complete in your body, you are the dharmakāya.
Supreme great vajra, homage to you, Heruka!
Body vast as the hosts of stars in the sky of the nature of reality,
Form like the massed rain clouds of summer,
Mouth open, speaking like the waves of the ocean,
Like the outspread rays of a hundred thousand suns,
Body perfected by arranging the nine great vehicles and all the phenomena of saṃsāra into a single mudrā,
Three kāyas spontaneously present, dharmakāya blazing with a thousand finials,
Wearing a crest of matted hair, dark brown,
Bathed by a hundred thousand nāga kings, who present every offering,
With vajra speech, clicking your tongue,
You proclaim the terrifying sounds hūṃ and phaṭ,
Words that proclaim the ultimate secret mantra of the Great Vehicle.
Blazing brows flicker like lightning in pitch darkness;
Red eyes with an averting glare blaze like suns;
From blazing nostrils the black whirlwind of the eon swirls,
Raining vajra thunderbolts and great hail of meteoric iron.
Baring sharp-edged fangs, clenched and gnashing,
You fall fiercely upon the hosts of afflictions and wrong views.
Skulls and bones, flesh and blood, marrow-bones, worms and pus
You devour with a crunching sound in your menacing, devouring mouth.
Venomous snakes, fresh hides and other terrifying ornaments
Adorn your menacing body with the greatest majesty.
Hosts of many menacing devourers and terrifiers
Serve as emanated messengers bearing blazing emblems.
All of saṃsāra and all the phenomena of liberation
Sport majestically as the seat of the Great Hero.
Without rival, the one great and mighty one alone,
To the blazing body of the Supreme Secret I bow and give praise."

Through this praise, all phenomena became fully complete in the body, and there was nothing that was not awakened.

Then again the Blessed One, the Lord of the Supreme Secret himself, became the palace in which all mudrās are arrayed as mudrās. This is called the meditative absorption of the Stream of Emanation.

Then the Blessed One, the Lord of the Supreme Secret, entered into the meditative absorption called the Stream of the Nature of Reality. He spoke this meditative absorption of the Stream of Dharma:

"On the volume of meaning that is space,
The hosts of stars are written as letters of awareness;
Proclaimed by the sound of the four great elements,
The stream of the Dharma of secret mantra is never cut off."

The meditative absorption called the Stream of Dharma is complete.

Then again the Blessed One, the Lord of the Supreme Secret, entered into the meditative absorption of the Stream of Nectar and spoke thus:

"Within the excellent bhandha of the trichiliocosm,
The great poison of the beings of the three realms of existence
Is compounded with the buddhas of the three times as its ingredients,
And so the maṇḍala of nectar is spontaneously perfected."

So he spoke. Then again the Blessed One, the sattva Vajragarbha, entered into the meditative absorption of the Stream of Offering and spoke thus:

"The requisites of the ocean of saṃsāra
Are the ornaments of an ocean of offerings;
The whole limitless ocean of existence
Sports as supreme Samantabhadra clouds of offerings."

So he spoke. Then again the sattva Vajragarbha entered into the meditative absorption of the Stream of Recitation and spoke thus:

"From saṃsāra, the dharmadhātu itself,
Dawns the buddha essence of sentient beings;
Sounds and voices beyond the reach of thought
Are all the words of the mantra of recitation."

So he spoke. Then again the sattva Vajragarbha entered into the meditative absorption of the Stream of Union and spoke thus:

"With saṃsāra, the mother wisdom herself,
The king of compassion and skillful means
Is joined without meeting or parting,
And so all are born as children of the Victors."

So he spoke. Then again the sattva Vajragarbha entered into the meditative absorption called the Stream of Liberation and spoke thus:

"These fields for the liberation of saṃsāra,
By the great weapon of blazing wisdom,
Are liberated as being without intrinsic nature,
And drawn forth into the great dharmakāya of equality."

So he spoke. Then again the sattva Vajragarbha entered into the meditative absorption called the Stream of the Reliquary and spoke thus:

"On Mount Meru of the desire realm as the foundation,
The form realm is the steps and the firm base of the throne;
The formless realm is the vase support together with the vase:
The great reliquary abides continuously as the dharmakāya."

So he spoke. Then again Sattvavajra spoke thus:

"Space, fire, water, earth and wind
Appear as the foundation, with Mount Meru as the support;
The tiers, together with the seven mountains,
Are themselves the steps and the firm base of the throne;
The mountains are the vase support together with the vase:
This too is the dharmakāya, never interrupted."

Again Sattvavajra spoke thus:

"The four branches of erecting it are the foundation and support;
The wheel of the great gathering is the firm base of the throne;
The first branch is the vase support together with the vase,
Draped with braided locks, diadems and garlands."

So he spoke. Then the sattva Vajragarbha entered into the meditative absorption called Fully Raising the Dead:

"The great method of accomplishment with a corpse:
In the places of the great charnel grounds,
Seek out the substances of the corpse,
And seal them with the mantra that transforms as one wishes.
Seated upon the corpse,
Imagining oneself as the corpse's antidote,
Recite the corpse mantra without interruption.
If luster and radiance appear on the corpse,
That is the sign that it will be accomplished;
Continue the meditative absorption without interruption.
When the corpse begins to stir,
Smear it with the gold-transforming elixir.
Binding all the limbs of the corpse,
Recite the mantra purely.
When the corpse itself rises up,
It displays the miracles of the corpse.
Then the person who keeps the discipline
Cuts off the corpse's tongue,
And if he strikes the corpse with it, it turns to gold.
Likewise, whatever one wishes,
If one transforms it by that condition, so it becomes.
With that weapon, the sword of the severed tongue,
One cuts off the corpse's bhandha,
And whatever substance one wishes is accomplished.
Taking the bhandha as armor
And bearing the sword of the tongue,
One can turn back even the battle of the asuras.
The swift feet of one who has accomplished the vetāla
Are beyond all measure:
Four men endowed with skill,
Facing the four directions,
At one moment shoot arrows into the distance,
And before the arrows fall to the ground
He catches all four out of the sky;
In a single instant he circles the world,
And before the men's hands have lost their warmth
He has arrived back at his place at once.
'From a corpse every desire arises,'
So the Victors of the three times have taught."

So he spoke. This is the meditative absorption of accomplishing the vetāla.

Then again the sattva Vajragarbha entered into the meditative absorption called the Vajra Ladder of Space and spoke thus:

"Having meditated on space as a vajra,
When one has gained the meditative absorption,
Climbing the vajra ladder
In a zigzag manner, this way and that,
And going into the expanse of space,
Look upon the world realms of appearance and existence.
Thereby the domain of the mind becomes vast."

So he spoke. Then again the sattva Vajragarbha entered into the meditative absorption called Pressing Down the Great Ocean and spoke thus:

"When the meditative absorption of the ocean
Filled with vajras has become clear,
Travel to the great ocean,
And the sentient beings dwelling in the ocean
Should be liberated by the vajra of meditative absorption."

So he spoke. Then again the sattva Vajragarbha entered into the unobstructed maṇḍala of wind and moon and spoke thus:

"On the liṅga of pure space,
Meditate on a moon maṇḍala at its center;
When the meditative absorption has become firm,
Travel to all the realms."

So he spoke. Then again the sattva Vajragarbha entered into the meditative absorption called the Fully Shining Sun and spoke thus:

"On the liṅga of pure space,
Meditate on a sun maṇḍala at its center;
When the meditative absorption has become firm,
Dispel all the places of darkness."

So he spoke. Then again the sattva Vajragarbha entered into the meditative absorption called Extinguishing the Burning Eon:

"On the tip of the yogin's threatening finger,
A moon maṇḍala blazes with clear light.
Meditate on it, and when the meditative absorption has become firm,
Let a stream of water fall and extinguish the fire."

So he spoke. Then again the sattva Vajragarbha entered into the meditative absorption called Hurling a Thousandfold World into the Distance and spoke thus:

"In the maṇḍala of the yogin's activity,
Meditate very clearly
On a thousandfold world with its encircling ring;
Then, to train thoroughly in skill in meditative absorption,
Hurl it into the realm of space,
And raise it from ground to ground."

So he spoke. Then Sattvavajra entered into the meditative absorption called Spreading a Hundred Thousand Sun Maṇḍalas and spoke thus:

"On the yogin's threatening finger,
From a blazing sun maṇḍala
A hundred thousand blazing light rays radiate;
From each one of those light rays
A hundred thousand sun maṇḍalas radiate,
And thereby all darkness is dispelled."

So he spoke. Then again the sattva Vajragarbha entered into the meditative absorption called the Great Element, the Stream of the Waters of the Eon, and spoke thus:

"From the wheel of the yogin's hand
The essence of the water moon dawns brightly;
As the stream of a river of nectar descends,
It quenches the great fire of the burning eon."

So he spoke. Then again Sattvavajra entered into the meditative absorption called the Blazing Mudrā of Body and spoke thus:

"On the blazing jewel liṅga
Is the glorious body of the wisdom of the buddhas;
On the eight facets of the blazing jewel,
The glorious, blazing mātṛkās of awareness
Shine clearly as eight,
And the root maṇḍala of wisdom is complete."

So he spoke. Then again the sattva Vajragarbha entered into the meditative absorption called the Blazing Mudrā of Speech and spoke thus:

"On the blazing lotus liṅga,
Eight syllables of blazing jewel
Blaze intensely, radiating and gathering:
This is the root mudrā of speech."

So he spoke.

Then again Sattvavajra entered into the meditative absorption called the Blazing Mudrā of Mind and spoke thus:

"On the blazing vajra liṅga,
Encircled by the syllable hūṃ,
And at the outer edge eight jewel syllables
Encircled by a deep blue color:
This is the secret wisdom of mind."

So he spoke. Then Sattvavajra spoke this sūtra of meditative absorption:

"The mind is of the nature of illusion;
As it is imagined, so it appears.
Even if it does not appear as the sūtra says,
Mind itself becomes as it is imagined.
Though those with material bodies and fixed focus do not see it,
Those with higher knowledge and bodies of mind do see it.
If one imagines for a long time in whatever way one imagines,
The fruit of that imagining, grown strong,
Will be found clearly, in direct perception.
The habitual tendencies of virtue and nonvirtue,
And the intrinsic natures of existence and nonexistence,
Are nothing but habitual tendencies grown strong.
Therefore meditative absorption, mind itself, is foremost."

So he spoke. This is the ninth chapter, on continuous meditative absorption, from the great king of tantras, the Vajra Array, the Sūtra of the All-Gathering Awareness.

Then the sattva Vajragarbha spoke this practice of the great secret union:

"For the yogins of the Great Assembly,
This most secret practice
Is the trail on which the Sugatas, the Victors, have gone,
And it is also the path for persons of the world.
Not realizing, realizing wrongly,
Realizing partially, not realizing the authentic itself:
Having cleared away these extremes, which are like wrong paths,
One enters the path of the Sugatas, the Victors.
Therefore this supreme path of practice
I shall explain to the gathered assembly.
Listen with the utmost respect."

Then the members of the assembly, having paid homage with the gesture of secret union, made this request: "Blessed One, great Lord of Secrets, please explain to us the secret of secrets, the great secret word, the marvelous wisdom, the authentic meaning of the trail on which the buddhas have gone." When they had requested this, the sattva Vajragarbha entered into the meditative absorption called Delightfully Entering the Assembly of Queens and spoke thus:

"The bhaga, the secret lotus,
Is the palace of the Sugatas, the Victors:
For the Victors of the past, the sphere where they came to rest;
For those present now, the support on which they abide;
For those who will come hereafter, the trail on which they go.
So it appears to the Victors.
Not knowing it thus is the path of saṃsāra;
Yogins, come to know it!
The supreme object to be taken here
Is the secret mother, auspicious and endowed with the marks:
Her eyebrows long, her fingers slender,
Her joints and eyes, it is said, are rounded and fine.
Her mouth bears the fragrance of the utpala;
Lovely, endowed with the marks, slender at the waist;
Her hair soft and sapphire blue, wound in tresses;
Teeth white and closely set, eyebrows long;
Eyes like the full-blown flax flower;
Forehead broad, hairline curving;
Nose fine, complexion clear: she is of the lotus type.
Body upright, neck long, with the bearing of a heroine;
The circles of her breasts like the full moon;
Flesh fair and firm, with graceful curves;
Unhurried, she walks with gentle steps;
Her voice sings like a young goose;
She has every graceful manner and poise.
The supreme man takes her as consort.

Consecrating her as consort:
Upon the aggregate of her form
The five seeds of the Sugatas
Become clear, radiating and gathering;
Having sealed, blessed and empowered her,
The supreme forms of the five Sugata mothers become clear.
For the vajra of method it is likewise.
When they have become clear in this way,
Meditate on a mountain of fire in the mother's space;
On the crescent moon at the center of the secret place,
Radiate and gather the bodhicitta.
Just as a reflection appears clearly,
So it is in the space of passion.
Unborn and unceasing,
Awareness, appearing of itself, is equality.
The sphere of space is utterly pure, the expanse of great bliss;
This is the teaching of non-abiding great bliss.
From the very nature of awareness and method,
Blessing becomes clear spontaneously, by its own power.

Her face is ruddy, likewise her shoulders;
Her forehead broad, her eyebrows long;
The luster of her eyes is bright, their shape long;
Her teeth like great conches, closely set, of crystal hue;
Her face long, her cheeks fine;
A sweet fragrance rises from her body,
And from her mouth rises the scent of camphor;
Her breasts are high at the center, swelling round.
She is known as the lotus woman.

The one taught as the conch woman
Is fair, clear with the luster of the moon;
Her face and body turn to the right like a conch;
Her topknot, sapphire blue, coils to the right;
Her voice is strong, melodious, clear in its words;
Small in stature, her body appears beautiful.

The girl called Long-Eyed
Is clear in wisdom and great in desire,
Firm in her discipline toward her lord;
Her secret moon is white and red, its breath heavy;
Pressing tightly and coiling,
No one can ever have enough of her warmth.
She is skilled in the means of hidden secrecy;
Outwardly and inwardly heavy like gold;
Free of conceptual thought, devoted to the meaning;
Her face is square in shape;
Her eyes long, slender and curving;
She is adorned with lovely ornaments of white and red.

Speaking of the type of the tree:
In the midst of a trunk with many branches,
Thick with leaves, beautiful in its boughs;
Her hair, by nature sapphire blue,
Is described as tresses, like the bodhicitta;
Above and below she is everywhere slender and fine,
Tall in stature and supple.
The signs of the one called Slender Sapling:
Twelve, twenty-five,
Untouched by the vajra weapon,
Endowed with the mula rakta;
Slender and fine, and long of face.

The elephant woman is yellowish and square-built;
Brownish, with bulging eyes and thick eyebrows;
Her hair brownish, her teeth large;
Her nose long, her cheekbones high and long.

The woman with markings
Is dark, triangular, with a coarse-haired head,
Wrinkled with anger, with a crooked manner,
A single trunk entirely without limbs.

"One lacking the cause of birth is inauspicious;
Untouchable, a branch scorched by fire.
Yet, although in general it is taught thus,
Those who have faith and keep the discipline,
Whether pleasing to the mind or not pleasing,
If, devoted in faith, they offer their bodies,
One should treat them all equally, as objects of compassion.
Whoever makes conceptual choices of rejecting and accepting,
Their obscurations are boundless.
In the marvelous secret offering,
Whoever performs the complete secret homage
Clears away all the obscurations of karma
And attains the secret focus of the pith instructions.

For the mother examined in this way,
With fragrant incense, sandalwood and so forth,
And water mixed with aloeswood,
Purify her by the rite of bathing.
With sweet fragrances and other things of many kinds,
Apply various unguents to her body.
Then also the great inner bath
Is to be made utterly pure:
With water of the substances and with mingled substances,
Purify the inner substances.
Sugarcane essence and the like,
All that have become supreme in taste,
Mixing them with elixir or with nectar,
One should combine the inner substances.

When the rite of substances is complete,
Generate the mind set on supreme awakening.
In order to train the intellect in the nonreferential Dharma,
The meaning of the root, the bodhicitta,
I shall explain at great length.
Since the aggregates appear as illusion,
It is certain that mind has no intrinsic nature.
Since appearance and existence are one in space,
It is certain that saṃsāra is illusion.
Since the all-ground is the treasury of phenomena,
It is certain that buddha is nowhere else.
Since there is no attachment even to love,
It is certain that the mind is not disturbed by hatred.
Since there is no fixed abode,
It is certain that there is no traversing of the grounds.
Since the mind has neither birth nor death,
There is no dread of transference and change.
Therefore all appears as illusion.
By knowing illusion to be without intrinsic nature,
All is the essence of the nature of reality.
When the stains of conceiving signs
Are washed in that river of the nature of reality,
The stains of grasping become pure.

Then the rite of adornment:
Since it is the equipment of the substances,
Flowing, fluttering robes and garments,
Jeweled clasps together with earrings,
Pendants, charming necklaces and the like,
Armlets, strings of pearls and garlands,
Adorned with gems, jewels and so forth,
Anklets, bracelets and the like,
Adorned with gold, silver and so forth.
With music, sounds and songs of many kinds,
One should bear various offering substances.

Then the rite of drawing her in:
When mingling through secret words,
By the samaya of the vajra tongue,
Indicate by signs in words.
When one says samaya ho,
Mingle also with samaya stvaṃ.
When one says ragayami,
One should say raho hṃ.
When one says tipasuki,
Indicate with gandecita.
When one says atipuho,
Indicate with pratipuho.
If one moves the right eyebrow,
Indicate with the left eyebrow.
If one signals with the right nostril,
Likewise signal with the left nostril.
If one signals the uṣṇīṣa at the crown,
Likewise signal at the liṅga of the śrīvatsa.
When the vajra liṅga radiates light,
The lotus stamens blaze, radiate and coil.
Thus mingle the meanings of these words.

As for the special rite of urging,
In meaning it is fitting to urge thus:
With the rays of the sun of wisdom,
Urge the light of the moon of supreme method.
When the rite of urging is complete,
Offer the articles of offering."

Then all the buddhas became clear in the state of the moon maṇḍala, and wisdom became clear in the state of the sun maṇḍala. Then with the rays of the sun they urged thus:

"King of the supreme Unmoving One, please heed us!
Nonconceptual, utterly stainless body,
Face like the full moon, radiant with rays of light,
Please arise for the sake of joyful play!"

When they had urged thus, from the rays of the moon came this urging:

"Lovely and rich one, sphere of the ocean of Victors,
Profound wisdom, radiance of the pure lotus,
Supreme lady of conduct, skilled in the ways of magical display,
Lovely and rich one, display your various miracles!"

So it was said. Then occurred the miracle called the Utter Offering of All the Hosts of Articles, and from the rays of the sun a sound arose:

"This blazing lotus stamen
I offer with the radiance of a smile;
From the miracle of the delighted vajra,
Please let the rain clouds of awakening pour down!"

From the rays of the moon arose the sound of miracles:

"Oh! Pleasure grove of the mind,
Garland of the longing heart, consort of wisdom,
Within the coiled amulet box of the lotus
The vajra of bliss blazes with awakening."

As this sound arose, it became the cause from which miracles of various mudrās arose.

Then the sublime Sattvavajra entered into the meditative absorption called Embracing All Saṃsāra with Compassion, and from the one called Supreme Method, the Moon Vajra, this sound of miracles arose:

"All the lords of desire
Are the mistresses of passion's proud swagger.
In order to purify passion,
Desire should be led into its proper place."

As this arose, all of saṃsāra was held in the alms bowl of great compassion.

Then Sattvavajra entered into the meditative absorption called Burning the Obscurations and spoke thus:

"On the blazing vajra liṅga,
Through the radiating light of the wisdom of a,
The firewood of the obscurations of passion
Is purified by the wisdom of fire."

Then again Sattvavajra entered into the blessing of the bhaga and spoke:

"All the phenomena that appear as saṃsāra
Are vividly clear in the essence of the bhaga;
The limbs of the mother's body
Are clear as the very nature of the five mothers."

So he spoke. Then the sattva Vajragarbha entered into the meditative absorption called Cutting Off All Attachment and spoke thus:

"If the nature of reality is realized as space,
However much union and liberation one performs,
It is a method for guiding passion and aversion,
And not even a particle appears."

So he spoke. Then all the buddhas entered into delighted play with the whole assembly of queens, and did not waver at all from nonreferential bliss. Then the great rain of bodhicitta, the essence of the lotus, fell without interruption. All of saṃsāra was born as heirs of the Victors. The Victors moistened cessation with bodhicitta and caused the sprout of existence to grow. Marvelous articles of offering appeared in an inconceivable, swaying profusion. All the vidyādhara sattvas, having purified the cognitive obscurations, were suffused within the dharmakāya.

Then Sattvavajra entered into the meditative absorption called Empowering All Beings:

"The immeasurable Victors
Arise from the cause, the sentient beings of saṃsāra.
If the stream of saṃsāra's sentient beings were cut off,
The stream of buddhas too would be cut off.
Whoever, knowing saṃsāra
To be of the nature of reality, speaks of it so,
That one is self-arisen wisdom of awareness,
A king of Dharma, an heir of the Victors."

So he spoke. Then the sublime Sattvavajra, knowing that all phenomena are accomplished by entering through the door of aspiration, spoke this path of the great ground of aspiration:

"These heirs of the Victors in the six classes of beings,
Who wander in non-realization and wrong realization,
Should be generated as heirs of the Victors,
And the streams of saṃsāra should be cut.
Placing them within sun and moon,
They are perfected on the sixteenth ground.
Therefore, with one of noble family and excellent marks,
A learned one endowed with the meaning,
Having perfected all the limbs of union,
On the crescent moon in the mother's sphere of space
Generates the accomplished celestial palace.
Its eight spokes clear as nine crescent moons,
From the bodhicitta the host of mudrās:
Generate the wisdom of the nine deities clearly.
By this, all becomes perfect."

So he spoke. This is the tenth chapter, on the practice of secret union, from the great king of tantras, the Vajra Array, the Sūtra of the All-Gathering Awareness.

Then the sattva Vajragarbha entered into the meditative absorption called Unchanging, Never Transferring, and spoke thus:

"Those yogins and vidyādharas
Who wish to abide without transference,
For one year, or for one month,
Or likewise for a number of days,
Fifteen, or else up to seven,
At dawn, at the start of the last watch,
The vidyādhara yogin practitioner,
Facing toward the east,
Draws within all that is gathered by the eastern sky,
Gods, nāgas and yakṣas, together with the world,
And, not letting that breath out again,
Should dissolve it into his own interior.
Finally, mingle it within the nose.
When one has practiced thus many times,
One obtains all great glory and splendor."

So he spoke. Then again the sattva Vajragarbha entered into the meditative absorption called Support and spoke thus:

"Whoever wishes to abide in a support,
In a supreme place with excellent signs,
Having gathered everything needed,
Should set out a platform made of earth.
On an eight-petaled lotus at the center,
A red crescent moon, with four gates,
Encircled at the edge by a vajra fence:
Arrange the glorious mudrā of life.
In a vase of precious design,
Having filled it richly with life substances,
Cover its mouth with a vessel bearing the marks.
Having bound it with the vajra cord,
Enthrone it as boundless life,
And likewise place it at the center.
On the four precious vases,
Arrange and place the four secret maidens.
On the vase that has arisen in wonder,
Draw the buddhas of the four families, father and mother,
And the sattvas of their respective retinues,
Joining method and wisdom,
In clusters in their places in each direction.
At the four gates place the four gatekeepers.
Having arrayed all the mudrās of mind,
In the amulet box of sun and moon, ayu
Should coil, blaze, shimmer and be clear.
ayu melts into bodhicitta.
She who bears the supreme victory banner, treasury of jewels,
She who confers empowerment, glorious, possessing the treasure store:
The secret maidens hold the treasure of life.
ayu radiates into the hearts of the four mothers,
And likewise passes in sequence through the clusters.
Gathering all the supreme vajra life,
Dissolve it into oneself with white and red light.
Then recite the vajra life garland.
In the west, in the supreme sphere of life,
In the realm of the Lotus Power,
Is the Loving One, the wisdom of Amitāyus.
In his heart, the great śrīvatsa,
In an amulet box of the light of the two clarities,
ayu of bodhicitta blazes.
That supreme life, clear red and blazing,
Radiates to the vidyādhara yogins.
Radiating, through the sublime nine openings,
It moistens the breath of the citta to radiance.
Having abandoned all six causes of life's decline,
Meditate on the five causes that give rise to splendor.
That ayu of bodhicitta,
Having been hidden in the unborn sphere,
Is explained at the end by seven mudrās:
The first is the seal of body, speech and mind;
The second, the blazing crossed vajra of suppression;
The third, the seal of glorious Paramāśva;
The fourth is the precious śrīvatsa;
The fifth is the golden guhya;
The sixth, on a red crescent moon;
The seventh blazes in an amulet box of sun and moon.
The three continua as the essence of life,
The celestial palace as the essence of the life force:
In the maṇḍala blazing crimson,
The red ayu radiating light
Establishes deathless life:
It is the unchanging svastika.
Since life is hidden in the sphere of the nature of reality,
Even if the Victors of the three times rose up as enemies,
There it could not be seized or pointed out.
Then one should act however one pleases."

This is the vajra practice of life.

Then the sublime Sattvavajra entered into the meditative absorption called the Vajra Wrathful One Who Steals All Life and spoke thus:

"Having made the maṇḍala of transformation blaze,
From the rays of the śrīvatsa at the heart
Of the blazing wisdom wrathful one,
Many goddesses of power radiate.
In their hands they hold white and red hooks,
Together with caskets.
With these they steal all the life
And all the splendor of the six classes of beings,
And, gathering them into the caskets,
Dissolve them into the vidyādharas of life.
Apply them as desired, as above."

This is the sūtra called Vajra Stealing of Splendor and Radiance. This is the eleventh chapter, called the Vajra Practice of Life, from the great king of tantras, the Vajra Array, the Sūtra of the All-Gathering Awareness.

Then the sublime Sattvavajra entered into the meditative absorption called Perfecting All the Assemblies. Having entered into it, he explained at length this maṇḍala called the Supreme Secret of the Charnel Ground:

"This sublime root maṇḍala
Of each and every maṇḍala,
In order to liberate all of saṃsāra,
I shall explain to the yogins.
Listen, host of the Great Assembly!
The supreme, sublime places of practice:
Having examined with certainty
The distinctions of their faults and qualities,
On the basis of that knowledge
One should apply them in two ways, adopting and rejecting.
The summit where great mountains gather,
The great slopes of white snow and crystal,
Is a place where lion cubs roam:
It is a place for pacifying everything.
In the midst of a mass of great vajra crags,
The great cave of a vast wilderness
Is a place where great beasts of prey roam:
It is a place for accomplishing great fierce rites.
A charnel ground, a desolate gorge,
A junction of three ways, a mountain of rugged ravines,
Is a place where charnel-ground beasts roam:
It is a place for accomplishing passion."

"Within a forest canopied with a net of foliage,
On a very fierce and fearsome slope,
Is a place where tigers and leopards roam:
It is auspicious as a place for accomplishing desires.
A confluence of rivers, a fearsome watery place, an island in the ocean,
Encircled on all sides by a fence of water,
Is a place where water beasts roam:
Therefore use it for the activity of non-action.
There, with perfect provisions,
Seeing to whatever is wanted, without hindrance,
Free of anything blameworthy,
With abundance, generate supreme diligence.
In order to repel, with the supreme essence,
The great illnesses that arise from the elements,
Nectars, or sacred essences that liberate,
Should be set out in full readiness.
Because obstacles may arise through the conditions
Of combined illnesses, seasonal illnesses
And illnesses caused by poison,
Arrange to have medicines and a skilled physician.
Skull cups together with braided locks,
Small amulet boxes and other articles,
Having endowed them with the great seal,
Make them supports from which siddhis arise.
The supreme practitioner, the secret yogin,
In order to cleanse minds of weariness
And to destroy the darkness of delusion,
Should gather in full readiness
Conches, staff drums and clay drums,
Hand drums, lutes and kokilas,
All the articles perfect in sweet sound,
Music that brings forth every variety.

The great assembly, utterly replete,
Is the master, the vajra wheel king.
At the great time of secret union,
The yogins practicing the great accomplishment
Generate compassion, love, joy and equanimity,
And the yearning bodhicitta.
Moreover, perfect in great skill in every respect
Through knowing and the knowable,
Vajra essence, supreme syllables:
The assembly, utterly replete,
Is the master, the vajra wheel king,
Who has become a treasury of supreme qualities,
Never exhausted though questioned for as long as existence lasts,
Able to withstand conditions, holding complete empowerments and transmissions,
Bearing the form of all vajras,
Gathering all, producing all, undisturbed,
Pervading all like the expanse of space,
Taking as consort the supreme mother, loving as a mother,
His mind-stream trained, holding the supreme discipline,
Unobstructed, free of doubt and hesitation,
Without grasping or attachment, without adopting and rejecting;
Steadfast, able to retain, a vajra kiṅkara,
Holding the right time of the vajra heat,
Applying the casting out and gathering in of words at the right moments,
Cutting the trajectory with waves of qualities,
Setting the crucial point on good and bad as his focus;
Having the melodious voice of vajra words,
His vajra vision utterly pure,
A lord of speech, skilled in words,
Completing the garland of activities.

As for the great time for applying this:
Examining with certainty and holding to the sūtras,
It should be known from the branches of the Vedas, well composed.
The auspicious days of the gods,
And the good conjunctions and junctures of time,
Are worldly conceptions of signs;
When examined, auspicious signs arise.
The first of the four divisions of time
Is supreme for fierce rites and vetālas.
Summer and autumn are auspicious;
Likewise apply them to pacifying and enriching.
Bringing under control too is power,
Said to belong to the great secret time of the corpse.
The seeking of wealth and all magical powers
Are applied pervading all four divisions.
As for the supreme substances of the great practice,
If their time has come,
This time is the great time of siddhi;
The learned never let this time slip by.
For yogins who are beginners in the activity,
When the desired signs
Arise pleasingly and the tokens are found,
It is the great time of the gathering of samaya and dhāraṇī.
When through training one has reached the culmination of skill,
And the qualities of knowledge and intellect have grown in abundance,
These have reached their full measure:
Examine closely this supreme time.

Thus from the coming together of the four causes
The result is accomplished in fact,
As one obtains jewels by relying
On a boat and a skilled ferryman.
All oceans, for as long as they remain,
All one in being without intrinsic nature,
Selfless, free of sides, spontaneously present:
In the one there are by nature no varieties.
When conventions are fully comprehended as pure,
That itself is the great definitive meaning,
And everything becomes pure.
Since self and other are not two,
Oneself is one's own nature;
What is called buddha is not elsewhere:
Oneself is the spontaneously present great sovereign lord.
Whatever things one desires
Are accomplished just as they are;
As with the turning of a potter's wheel,
However one focuses, so it becomes.
In the realm of beings, great in splendor,
Wielding power over the five great elements,
Special marvelous abilities
Will arise without doubt.
The practices of those who strive thus,
Substances, provisions and so forth,
Knowledge and qualities, are perfect:
Since everything is completely present,
With nothing to be reached for elsewhere,
That is what is called perfect.
These activities of practicing the great secret
Are carried out in accord with the actions of saṃsāra,
And though they appear as conduct of the five afflictions,
Just as light makes darkness visible,
Faults become great qualities,
And deceits and flaws become great siddhi.
Thus, by turning back the channel of saṃsāra,
One swirls into the ocean of non-abiding wisdom.

The great importance of examining the excellent site:
Examine it for resemblance to the sky, a wheel, a vajra,
A jewel with eight facets, the sphere of a lotus,
A crossed vajra and so forth.
The ground too is likewise:
Stacked mountains, receding slopes,
And likewise raised and replete forms:
The learned should examine their resemblance.
Furthermore, the distinctions of the families,
Five and so forth, each in turn:
Having understood their respective places,
Accomplish that precious jewel.

The faulty places to be avoided:
The ridgepole tip of malicious Yama,
The claw scratches of a fierce mātṛkā,
The tear tracks of a weeping rākṣasī,
The rockslide trail of a wrathful yakṣa,
Bhūtas, thieves, dangling growths and the like:
The faults of places, enemies and thieves,
The learned should certainly avoid.
Free of what is blameworthy
And of dangers that arise from broken vows,
Impure provisions, like a malicious one come into one's lap,
Should be cast off with the long cry of the great discipline of the fox.

In the region of a great wilderness,
Or where auspicious signs arise,
Dwelling in a pleasing place
Where joy will arise,
In order to fully pacify disputes,
The yogin endowed with compassion,
So as to bring the steadfast practice to completion,
Commands by the truth, or else requests,
The king of the place together with his retinue:
'Heed us, compassionate Victor deities,
Gods, nāgas and yakṣas with your hosts and armies!
Snow mountain peaks and jewel mountains,
Pleasure gardens of delight and parks,
Lakes, ponds, springs and places of flowing water:
All you who have become lords
Of each and every world,
Since I, the lord of yogins, myself,
Would make this ground spontaneously accomplished,
When I request the ground of accomplishment in Jambudvīpa,
Be seated here with your retinues
And grant the ground of precious treasures!'
Having said this, with various jewels
Placed in the ground, one should please them."

"Through this it becomes one's own,
And one is free of that fault.
In stirring the conduct of awakening
And generating the joy of merit,
O wise son of the one with magical power,
Do not fear, and never let jealousy arise.
When the practitioner of the supreme yoga
Has completed the mudrā of subduing Māra,
Against the assaults of the hosts of Rudra
The man recounts the aims of the activity
And declares it for the sake of the lord of the aim:
'When all the Victor lords
Rightly accomplished the supreme aim,
Through the special prowess of the man,
You who now know and bear witness, who discern the focus:
Just as of old the great glorious Lord of Dance
Liberated the four classes of Rudra,
Spread the secret teaching,
And with compassion liberated the six classes of beings,
So now, when I join the yoga to this ground,
Goddess, take note of it,
And when I travel to the ground of great bliss,
Should assaults of Rudra arise,
You who have eyes, exercise your power!'

With the special supreme activity of practice,
For all the activities to be undertaken,
In order to overcome signs by signs,
Examine the signs of the five tastes.
If they turn out virtuous, dwell there.
If they do not turn out auspicious,
The man skilled in means
Should apply the activity of suppressing or of moving.
There, so that it may hold a treasury of merit,
With substances such as the five precious things
Enrich the ground as a great treasure:
This is praised as supremely auspicious.
With earth, fat and the juices of the five great fragrances
Mixed together, make it square on all sides,
Like the shape of an extensive maṇḍala;
By one endowed with samādhi, mudrā and mantra,
All the objects of signs are purified,
And it is made a support where the Victors dwell.
To make it the source of vajra blessing
And to make the places utterly stable,
Through the pure supreme yoga
Take possession of the ground and bless it.
Having purified the ground there,
On the occasions of marking out the area with lines,
In that palace where the Victors dwell,
The great kiṅkaras of the fierce vajra
Fumigate with the smoke of black lcepok.
Having made it unbroken,
Reciting the mantras of the fierce wrathful ones,
Circle with the stances of the vajra wrathful ones.
With pastes of the two kinds of substance
In the manner of the wisdom mother's flowing,
Mixed with silk cotton,
Make moon drops everywhere.
With the Vajra King and the noble ones,
Applying their respective mantras and root mudrās
And their respective postures and stances,
Bless those places.

In order to enter the secret meaning,
Before the secret activities,
Entrust to the protectors who guard the teaching
The great activities of offering gifts, honoring and protecting.
Outside the place of the celestial palace,
As supports where the protectors dwell,
From precious grains, or from ore,
From wood or from bone,
Make cast images or forms of the mudrās,
Enthrone the protectors as four,
And make them the gatekeepers and guards of the place.
'From each side of Meru and the four continents,
Through the might of the power of Vajra, the Lord of Secrets,
Bound by oath to protect the world,
Four classes of greatly powerful protectors,
Skilled in taming your own kinds of obstructors,
Dharma protectors, enjoyment bodies,
Having perfected the four kinds of activity,
The colors of the four families each distinct,
Bearing the deities' emblems as your own signs,
Magically powerful guardians of the outer circle:
Through our yogic training in skill,
Make each of your retinues hold firm!'
In order to bring this prowess of practice to culmination,
To clear away the obstacles of conditions,
And to practice the secret pith instructions,
Suppress the intruding spirits and the samaya-breaking sri demons.
In the places of the great charnel grounds,
In the secret places of the yogin,
When dust enters the water of siddhi,
Gathering all the wrathful ones of the great hūṃ,
Perform the activities of killing and suppressing:
Cast them beneath the foundation of great Meru,
Give them as food to Yamarāja,
Send them into the mouth of the blazing makara,
Suppress them with the mudrā of the blazing wheel.
Their lineage destroyed to the seventh generation,
They can never rise again.
If the bodhicitta is born in them, release them.
vajra muṣṭi vaṃ
mama vaśaṃ kuru nan
suṃbha ni suṃbhā nāṃ
Thus, through the fierce application,
In the intermediate chamber outside the gate,
Having made a hole like space,
With poison and coarse powders,
Various kinds of blood and human corpses,
Smear thoroughly and suppress.

Then, within the vajra enclosure,
Looking with the lion's gaze,
Moving in the directions with vajra steps,
Looking outward with blazing wrathful eyes,
Majestic in a swaggering manner,
Raising the blazing vajra,
Clear away the great dust outside and inside.
hūṃ In the world of the great trichiliocosm,
Beings, plants, trees and forests,
And whatever exists, is seen, appears or is heard,
All are blazing blood drinkers:
Greatly terrifying, fierce, squat and blazing.
The charnel-ground maṇḍala is spontaneously present.
rakṣa yakṣa heruka
rakta krodhi jvala kha
hala hala tiṣṭha phaṭ
With this, by the stances of wrathful play,
The terrifying manner of the mātṛkās,
And the terrifying stances of the gathered phramen,
Circle the blazing maṇḍala.
Garlands of massed vajras,
Blazing hammers and hooks,
Wheels, swords and other implements:
Surround it with the emblems of all the mudrās.
From those, blazing mudrās,
And fire spreading, blazing from the eyes:
With this garland of blazing firelight,
Applying it in visualization or in actuality,
From the syllables of fierce blaze,
Fill the tent with the great glory of burning.
hūṃ raṃ The vajra mountain of fire:
On the eight blazing peaks of Meru,
Having struck down the eight blazing gods,
Is the abode of the greatly powerful vajra attendants.
With utterly unbearable blaze,
Greatly fierce hala, of terrifying form,
Having flayed the Great God (Śiva) and Viṣṇu,
He wears garments of human skin and hide;
In his hand he brandishes the great corpse club
Of blazing Yama and Matraṃ;
Many phramen, ninety thousand,
A blazing host, packed thick to the very end.
Displaying whatever tames each one,
With garlands of fire of the blazing citta,
Strung with necklaces and garlands at the throat,
The expanse of the blazing fire mass of the eon
Swirls and radiates up to the peak of existence.
All the realms of space are packed with masses of fire;
Vajra wheels radiate tongues of flame.
In the regions of charnel grounds and wilderness,
On the blazing mountain of fire,
None but the fire pretas
Can bear to remain.
The blazing flames above
Radiate and churn downward;
The blazing flames below
Coil over and surge upward.
Nine garlands of blazing flames
Roar and crackle as they fight and churn.
The charnel-ground fire blazes dark maroon:
Destroying aversion, it swirls dark blue;
Burning pride, it radiates dark yellow;
Equalizing desire, it radiates dark red;
Burning jealousy, it coils dark green;
Clearing away delusion, it coils dark maroon.
It is packed with wheels of the expanse of great blaze."

jvala maṇḍala hala hala raṃ
With the mudrā of the fire-mountain hand and of water,
One should encircle the site or the maṇḍala.
On the sky where the nature of reality lies open,
Stack up the great elements of the five wisdoms.
On the sky where the all-ground lies open,
Stack up the five great elements of the generating mothers.
From bruṃ arises a blazing jeweled palace,
Without edge or center, pervading everything,
Rising like a palm tree and hung with golden bells,
With a red crescent moon, the seal of magnetizing.
bruṃ khaṃ traṃ khalati bruṃ
With this, using the ointment of five kinds,
One should sprinkle and anoint all around.

The yogins who guard the supreme accomplishment,
Having set it in place with vajra words,
Bless the children of the Victor, the vidyādharas, the wheel of the command,
So that they may reach the result.
That great Vajra Lord of Victors
Makes the syllables that burn away obscurations
Into a garland and radiates them into the site.
Placing them upon a sun maṇḍala,
Seal them with the signs of male and female vidyādharas.
Sprinkle everything with the ointment of five kinds.
Give the mudrā of the emblem into the hand.
Practice in the manner of Mahāyoga.
Since all the ways of upholding the world
Are illusion, utterly,
Practice the conduct that transcends the world
Just as it arises.

How wonderful! In this marvelous realm,
This pleasure grove of the secret charnel ground,
You fortunate lords, children of the Victor,
Vidyādharas gathered here in assembly,
How wonderful! Companions in supreme joy and great bliss,
Bound by the life-cord of samaya,
Joined in skillful means and wisdom,
Shaking out the wings of emanation and spreading your pinions,
Soaring aloft by the strength of the two accumulations,
It is fitting that you glide in the sky of the nature of reality.
a yaṃ ja laṃ khaṃ together.
From this day on, children of the Victor,
Since you are lords, enjoy as you please.
To produce the enjoyments of yoga
And to turn poisons into nectar,
With the wisdom born from māṃ,
The substance in the copper vessel is to be digested and tamed.

The samaya substances, articles and enjoyments
Should from the outset be prepared in three separate portions,
Each kept apart, unmixed.
With the outer portion,
Prepare the articles for setting out offerings and gifts.
From the second of those two portions,
Make the enjoyment of the royal Dharma at the close.
As for the marvelous inner portions,
Having made them all into one,
Gather them on cloth or a wooden tray
And cover them with the substances of accomplishment.
Sprinkle them with the great ointment.
Fumigate them with the smoke of black incense.
With the mudrā of the vajra dance
And a blazing gaze, make it majestic.
Circle around it with great striding steps.
Drive off the strong odor of the various burnt offerings.
Then, through the blessing of the king,
Those articles are gathered into the essence
Of the five nectars, the samaya substances.
Upon them, from the radiating and gathering of the five seed syllables,
They melt and blaze as the five kinds of hand emblems,
And melt into great, pure nectar.
The wisdom goddesses, the vidyādharīs,
Four of them, or else eight kinds,
Being by nature the substances or articles,
Lift up the supreme food with dance
And present it with this song of displaying enjoyments:

"We vidyādharīs, the wisdom women,
Hold the accomplishment of the deities for the children of the Victor.
Enjoyments that satisfy with delight,
Like a wish-fulfilling jewel,
A wish-granting tree, a wish-granting cow,
Or crops that flourish without plowing:
May these great enjoyments of yoga,
Crowding the whole realm of space,
With all the vajra assemblies of delight gathered together,
Be emanated in abundance by the fortunate!"
Mastering the vajra dance,
The goddesses, in their charming way of bestowing,
Bowing and remaining in attendance,
Delight them with a host of music.
oṃ śrī vajra lāsyai hūṃ hūṃ vajra mālai trāṃ trāṃ vajra gīti hrīḥ hrīḥ vajra nṛtya aḥ aḥ
By the supreme means of vajra activity,
This vajra song brings forth splendor.
This enjoyment of vajra nectar
Is the heart of the essence of the supreme elixir,
The best enhancer of splendor and radiance,
The glory of luster, of supreme form and of mindfulness.
By the power of the blessing of the Vajra Holder,
Let it be food for the fortunate;
For the unfortunate it turns to poison,
Just as milk does for a snake.
samaya jaḥ kusucamama

"Goddess of purple dew, soft and clear,
You who turn all moisture into nectar,
Holder of the accomplishment of streams of nectar,
Send down a stream of water as the karmic share of those who tend you.
You who turn the leaves and petals of trees into nectar,
Holder of the accomplishment of the various grain crops,
Ripen into fruit the karmic share of those who tend you.
Goddess of firm stability, mistress of power,
Through whom juices and essences become nectar,
Holder of the accomplishment of the great molten nectar,
Make firm the karmic share of those who tend you.
Vajra goddess of crystal hue,
You who turn the rays of the sun into nectar,
Holder of the accomplishment of clarity and radiance,
Bring forth splendor in the karmic share of those who tend you.
Goddess of wrathful frown, deep blue,
You who turn the substances of the charnel ground into nectar,
Holder of the accomplishment of the various great meats,
You who send down blessings on the karmic share of those who tend you.
Goddess of elixirs, Intelligent One,
You who turn the elixir harvest into nectar,
Holder of the accomplishment of all supreme elixirs,
You who satisfy the karmic share of those who tend you.
Goddess of medicine, stream of wealth,
You who turn poisons into nectar,
Holder of the accomplishment of nectar medicine:
All these karmic shares of those who tend you,
Relying on the vajra song,
As the articles of Mahāyoga,
Enjoy them in a festival of music!"

Then, at this stage of the activity,
With the Dharma mudrā of Yama,
Who abides by the command, conforming within and without,
Bring him under control to do the work of a servant
And empower him to guard the outer boundary.
Whether in relief, cast, or drawn,
Whatever likeness has been made,
Outside the door of the outer courtyard
Place it upon a buffalo throne,
Please him with the suitable substances,
And then charge him with these words:

"From the palace of great secret spontaneous presence,
From the palace of Yama of karma,
The Blessed One, the Lord of Dharma, has declared:
Likewise, when the vidyādhara practitioner
Accomplishes this great scripture of the secret word
And enters with certainty into the true meaning,
These are precious jewels.
All who oppose and obstruct it,
The eight classes of gods and demons, and all bhūtas,
Are your retinue and servants,
So pronounce on them the Dharma of karmic maturation!
Until the yogin of the supreme secret
Has attained the unsurpassed result,
Do not let them pass beyond this ground;
Do not transgress the command of the Great Glorious Invincible One.
We are nondual from the very beginning,
One in the expanse of meaning just as we are.
At the outer border that you guard,
Hold firm to the vajra pledge!"
vajra yama rāja samaya stvaṃ
With this, Yama is bound by oath and commanded;
Should he transgress, he himself will burn.
The great activity that is to be undertaken,
The support that brings forth accomplishment,

Of the great mudrā and the supreme form,
Is accomplished when the platform has been well prepared.
For the materials compounded of colored powders,
Which serve to guide through symbols,
I shall explain the maṇḍala of images;
I shall explain it, examining each kind in turn.
In a small chamber, stable and smooth-surfaced,
Make a form in five colors,
In relief, evenly proportioned and tall in stature;
That bestows the accomplishment of signs.
If, on tree bark, on cloth, or on the shroud
Of the charnel ground, the great field, one draws in five colors
The blazing form of the mudrā deity,
That too is a support from which signs arise.
From materials such as stone and crystal,
Conch or ivory,
When the skilled have carved it in relief,
It becomes a support from which signs arise.
From materials such as gold, silver and jewels,
Copper, iron and bronze,
If a craftsman casts it without flaw,
That too becomes a support for signs.
From the plakṣa, the bodhi tree and the like,
From the supple branches of trees of good lineage,
If that form of the mudrā deity is well fashioned,
That too the learned declare to be a support.
On the finest of soft and pliant materials,
Well fashioning the face and the rolled folds,
If one makes the form of the great mudrā,
That too becomes fit to be a support.
On materials such as these,
Write upon the substance syllables, or emblems and signs,
Or names, or drops;
When accomplished, it becomes a support for signs.

Of these, what is explained here for now
Is how the signs that rest on the platform
Are examined and taken up for the sake of accomplishment.
The method of undertaking it is this:
On the materials mentioned above,
Make the platform beautiful and level,
Square on all sides with equal portions,
And with a compound of the five nectars
Make its center like a mirror.
With substances that are manifest and arisen, truly blessed,
Substances of self-arisen wisdom, accomplished,
In the marvelous palace of the secret command,
Smear and anoint it with the five substances of the charnel ground.

I shall explain in detail
The rite of laying out the lines with the vajra cord.
Secret willow fibers twisted many times,
Vajra threads and the like,
Long or short, for the encircling of the maṇḍala,
Not tangled into knots,
Triple-twisted, even, and of proper measure,
Are clear as the nature of the five wisdoms.
The method for blessing that material:
From the radiating and gathering of the two syllables
Of skillful means and wisdom joined,
Golden garlands of white and red light radiate.

Now, by the secret vajra method,
The consort who draws forth the bodhicitta and spins the thread
Is examined with certainty and taken here.
The secret consort, auspicious and endowed with the marks,
Has long eyebrow lines and slender fingers;
Her joints and eyes are said to be rounded and fine.
From her mouth rises the fragrance of the utpala,
And her body has the scent of camphor and sandalwood.
Lovely, endowed with the marks, narrow at the waist,
Her hair is soft and deep blue, coiling to the right.
Her teeth are white and close-set, her lips both white and red.
Her eyes are long, her eyebrows finely drawn,
Her eyes like a blooming flax flower.
Her forehead is broad, her hairline curving;
Her nose is fine, her complexion clear: she is of the lotus family.
Her body is straight, her neck long, with the bearing of a heroine.
Her breasts are high and full like the full moon.
Her flesh is fair and firm, inclined to embrace.
She is unhurried within, and her steps are gentle when she walks.
Her voice calls like a young goose.
She has a graceful bearing in every way.
The supreme man takes her as his consort.

As for blessing her as the consort:
In five places of the consort's aggregates,
The five seeds of the sugatas
Become clear through radiating and gathering;
Blessed and empowered by the buddhas,
The supreme bodies of the five sugata consorts become clear.
So too for the vajra father.
When they have become clear in this way,
Meditate on a fire mountain in the consort's space.
In the secret crescent moon of the breath,
Radiate and gather the bodhicitta.
Just as a reflection appears clearly,
So it is in the consort's space.
Then, with these vajra words,
Instruct the consort of the body with certainty:

"Wisdom goddess, secret consort,
Great magical woman who transforms into all desires,
In the manner of passion you bow to skillful means,
Entwining on the left and embracing on the right,
Joining the crescent moon with the radiance of your smile,
Drawing forth the secret bodhicitta.
You, goddess, are a child of the Victor,
Endowed with the marks of the nature of things;
Since you are the consort of vajra activity,
Perform all the secret activities!"

In the palace of the wheel of the hand,
Arrange an assembly of blazing sun and moon,
